# Changelog

All notable changes are documented here, in [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) format.
Versions are calendar dates, `YYYY.M.N`: the Nth release of that month. Anything that breaks a
file format, the CLI or the plugin protocol is marked **Breaking:**. Releases up to 1.17.0 used
semantic versioning.

Each release is published to [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/dip497/hivemind/releases) with prebuilt artifacts (`hive-linux-x86_64`, `hivemind-<version>-x86_64.AppImage`, `hive-darwin-arm64`, `hivemind-<version>-arm64-mac.zip`).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- A view can draw an agent's state the way the app does, with the same shape and word: `STATUS_MARKS` and `statusMarkSvg(tone)` in `@hivemind/view-sdk`. The app's own Layers, headers and notifications draw from the same table.
- The phone page in a browser reaches every computer of yours, not only the one it paired with: Home shows what waits on you across all of them. Pairing with someone else's computer adds an account instead of replacing yours; switch between accounts from a chip on Home or from Devices.
- A view that draws its own toolbar (`"toolbar": "off"`) can open the app's own menus from its buttons (view protocol 1.15, feature `hostMenu`): `hm.commands.openHostMenu("views", rect)` lists the views to switch to beside its button, and `"add"`, `"appearance"` and `"settings"` open Add, the theme panel and Settings. No permission: the person chooses in the menu.
- A view can ask for widget cards down the left edge instead of the right, with `"widgets": "left"` in its `hivemind-view.json`, so they stay off a terminal it docks on the right. A card you moved stays where you put it.
- A photo or video wallpaper no longer shows bright square blocks while it moves: its brightness is now a dark layer over it, not a filter on the moving picture.

- Install on a HiveHub plugin's own page installs it, after the same review as the list and the Add menu, instead of only going back to the list.
- An agent's status has a shape as well as a colour in the surface bar, Layers, a terminal's header and a frame's corner: working, needs you, finished, failed, idle and exited each look different, also with reduced motion on.
- A screen reader hears a tile's name with "needs you", "finished" or "failed" once when it comes to that state: for the same changes, and under the same **Settings → Notifications**, as a toast, and for the selected tile too.
- Two presets, **Clean** and **Clean Light**: Signal made flat, the same graphite and the same one warm colour for what waits on you, with glass and the background off; choosing another preset after them brings your glass and background back. Clean Light is its light twin and the first light look: the page, terminals, the editor, diffs, diagrams, notifications, the window behind the page and the system's own menus and scrollbars all turn light (`hive theme use clean-light`).
- Settings → Appearance has **Corners**, **Interface font** and **Code font**. The corner and font settings now change the app: before, `appearance.radius`, `uiFont` and `monoFont` were saved but drawn nowhere. The interface now speaks in your system's own font, as the design system decided (Geist stays a choice under **Interface font**).

### Changed

- **Breaking:** a view's own keys (`keys` on a command) no longer act while you type in a terminal or a field: the key is the terminal program's there.
- `hive theme export` and Settings → Appearance → **Copy JSON** leave out the wallpaper's photo or video and the overlays, and say so: they are files on your computer the recipient does not have, and an older wallpaper's address named your home folder. `hive theme import` and **Import…** keep the photo, video and overlays you have, take a whole settings.json as well as a theme file, and read only the look from any file. What a file leaves out, such as `mode` and `pluginSurfaces`, stays as you have it, and a file with none of the look in it (a package.json picked by mistake) is refused (`INVALID_THEME`; **That file is not a hivemind theme.**) and changes nothing. `hive theme export <file> --json` adds `leftOut`.
- **Breaking:** `hive config set` refuses a path that is not a setting in this version (`NOT_FOUND`, exit 5), and says which settings are there and the one you may mean (`appearance.glass.blur` for `appearance.blur`); before, it exited 0 and stored nothing. Under `views.chrome` and `views.toolbars` any view's id is a setting, and under `plugins.state` any plugin's `kind:id`.
- A widget's `hive.notify` follows Settings → Notifications (the switch, **Native OS popups** and **Do Not Disturb**), shows at most one notice every 10 seconds per widget, and cuts its text to 200 characters.
- **Breaking:** for tool authors: a tool's tile runs in the app's page and gets only the browser rights the app uses, notifications and writing to the clipboard. The camera, the microphone, the location and reading the clipboard are refused; open a page that needs them in a Browser tile, which asks.
- Settings → Shortcuts is made from the app's own keymap, every key in it, and shows each key as your computer writes it (⇧⌘A on a Mac, Ctrl+Shift+A elsewhere). The key hints in tooltips, menus and the Browser, editor and commit boxes do the same instead of showing ⌘ on every computer. An editor, a diff and a plan size their text with the terminal's font keys, the ones their buttons name.
- Status words are one list: Layers says "approve?", "input?" and "review?" as a terminal's header does, and "needs you" for a frame.

### Fixed

- A tool's diff (`PatchView`: Git history, Pull requests, Worktrees) draws added, removed and changed lines in the preset's own colours and its code in your code font, as the Diff tile does; before, it kept the diff library's green, red and blue and its own font in every preset.
- Issues reads in every preset. On Clean Light, label chips were pale text on a grey smudge and an assignee's initial a pale letter on a pastel disc; they are drawn for the page they are on. Each state's word reads at 4.5:1 on Clean Light and on every dark preset (on the state picker, Todo, In review, Backlog and Cancelled were 1.8 to 3.9:1 under Nord, Dracula, Solarized Dark and One Dark), and a done criterion's tick shows on its green.

### Security

- A site in a Browser tile asks before it uses your camera, microphone or location, shows notifications, or opens a link in another program (`mailto:`, `zoommtg:`, `slack:`): the question shows over the page, names the site, and its **Allow** and **Block** take a click only after half a second on screen. Your answer holds for the site until you quit the app. Before, every site got them without asking. **Site permissions**, by the address, allows a site ahead of asking (one that only checks never asks) or takes a **Block** back. Links that run code or open files (`file:`, `javascript:`, `search-ms:`) never open, and a site never gets USB, serial or input devices, a speaker or your screen. As in Chrome, a site does not ask to hold the keys in full screen (holding Esc gives them back), keep the screen on or keep its data; anything else it asks for is named in words (your computer's fonts, devices on your network, files you give it), and on a shared host the site's owner stays in its name (`alice.github.io`). A view, or a page a tool shows in its tile, never gets any of them.
- Known gap: with **Agent browser control** on, an agent can give a site these rights over the Chrome DevTools Protocol without asking you.

### Fixed

- Focusing a button or box in a tile that runs past the canvas's edge no longer shifts the canvas for a moment, which could make a click land on something else (seen on a Browser tile's Block and Allow).
- A form in a view submits, in the app and on the phone page: Enter in its text box fires the form's `submit` event, which did nothing before. The form still cannot leave the view's page.
- A look that is no built-in preset (an imported theme file) shows as **Custom** in Settings → Appearance instead of as Ubuntu, and **Reset** puts the terminal back to Signal's colours. The Accent's description says what it colours.
- An agent whose workers finish one after another is told "Your agents sent you messages" once, at its prompt, even before it has finished a turn since the app started; before, the line could be pasted into its prompt again for each worker.
- Shift+Enter in a terminal adds a new line in an agent's prompt instead of sending it. A program that reads keys by the kitty keyboard protocol gets Shift+Enter, Ctrl+I and Tab as themselves, also after a window reattaches.
- Esc in a full-screen terminal reaches the program instead of leaving full screen; ⌘/Ctrl+⇧+F leaves, and the full-screen buttons of a terminal, an editor and a diff name that key instead of Esc. Settings → Shortcuts lists the terminal's own keys.
- An agent whose background subagents never reported their end no longer shows "working" forever: two minutes after the last subagent signal they stop counting, now on the daemon that runs the agent.
- A setting a newer version added directly under a section of settings.json (`appearance`, `views`, `agents` and the others) survives a change made by this version; before, any change erased it. One added inside a group this version knows, such as `appearance.glass`, is still dropped. A guest who joins your workspace still gets only the named parts of your look.
- A widget's `tint` colours its card only when it is a note colour (`yellow`, `peach`, `pink`, `violet`, `blue` or `green`); any other word, `ink` included, drew dark text on a card of no colour and now leaves the card plain. `hive plugins check` reports such a tint.
- A frame's corner, Layers and a terminal's header agree about an exit: only a failed one is red, and a clean one raises no mark. A terminal that could not start, or whose session could not be opened, is failed too, also to a custom view when it closes, and Layers shows a failed tile without the blue mark for a finished one. A frame's corner also shows a plan or an approval waiting on you.
- An agent that waits for you to review its plan raises a toast, an OS notification and a screen reader's line, as its amber mark says it needs you; before, only a permission, a question or a blocked agent did. A supervised worker waiting for its lead's approval stays quiet: its lead answers it. **Settings → Notifications** names them under **Needs you**, and says under **In-app toasts** that it also covers what a screen reader says.
- A workflow's background workers no longer each raise a "finished" toast and notification when their turn ends, as was meant; one that needs you still does.
- Increased contrast (the system setting) strengthens faint text and lines with glass off too, not only with glass on.
- Settings → Shortcuts and the guide showed ⌘Tab for the next tile, which is Ctrl+Tab on a Mac too; Settings → Agents named ⌘\ for starting the default agent, which is ⇧⌘A / Ctrl+Shift+A. On Windows and Linux they no longer offer Super+B for Layers, which the system takes; the everyday keys come before Quit.

## [2026.10.5] — 2026-10-21

- A terminal or agent that fails to start says why (the daemon's own error, or that it stopped answering) instead of "not running on the machine it belongs to", and a late answer no longer streams into a tile marked exited.
- Agents started while the computer is busy get your shell's PATH and keys: a login shell slow to answer at startup is asked again in the background instead of being replaced by another shell for the rest of the session.
- After an update, a background process too busy to answer quickly is now still replaced when it speaks an older protocol, and the app says so when it will not stop, instead of carrying on with it.
- Quitting while the background process is busy now asks what to do instead of quitting as if nothing ran, and "Stop everything" now always stops it.
- Model and option lists that took too long to read from an agent's CLI are read again next time instead of staying empty until the app restarts.
- `hive plugins install`, `remove` and `rollback` say "app not running" only when it is not; when the app fails to load the plugin they show its error, and the new `hive plugins reload` asks it again.
- When the app cannot read the tool plugins installed here, their tiles stay as they were instead of all showing "not installed", and Settings → Plugins → Tools says why.
- Terminals and agents slow to start, as when the app comes back after an update with many open, are waited for while their daemon is still there, instead of being marked exited after a fixed few seconds.
- A terminal on a machine whose hive is older than the app now says to press Update hive in Machines, instead of starting its agent again over plain ssh while the original runs on there unseen.

## [2026.10.4] — 2026-10-20

- Search finds every word you type, at the start of a word, closest match first: `git pr` finds Pull requests, the same in Settings → Plugins, the Add menu, `hive plugins search` (new) and on HiveHub. The Add menu's search also lists tools and widgets from HiveHub you have not installed, with Install; HiveHub's list in Settings shows 25 a category with Show more, and long branch and remote folder lists show the first ones with Show more.
- Guide: **View as Markdown** and **Copy page** open the page's Markdown again (they went to `/guide/guide/…`), and **Open in ChatGPT, Claude, Cursor or Scira AI** hands the agent the page's Markdown. A how-to's prompt box offers the same agents under **Open in…**, next to **Copy prompt**.
- Write a package from `hive` alone: `hive plugins new package <name>` starts one with a PROMPT.md, `hive plugins new <kind> <name> --in <package>` and `hive plugins add <folder> --to <package>` add members (another owner's id is refused), and `hive plugins check <package>` checks every member with its own check, each problem at its file and line. The guide has a new Packages page.

- hive-net starts on a machine with no network route, such as one offline or in a container with no network: before, it stopped at once saying `Cannot bind to IPv4 or IPv6`, so the app could not pair or share there.
- Switches read on or off at a glance: on is a light track, off a dark one.
- An install from a review installs exactly what was reviewed, from the window or tab that asked for it: it is refused if the files, the version it replaces or its switch changed since. The audit log says when a person acted from a browser tab.
- **Settings → Plugins** has a Packages tab after Tools: a package of several plugins is one row with one switch, opened to its members, each with its kind, whether it is sandboxed or runs in the app, and its own switch. Its members stay in their kinds' tabs marked "Part of <package>". **Add → Add from a folder** reviews any folder first, as `hive plugins install --dry-run` does: every member, where it runs and what it asks for; an update lists what changed in each member and why it switches the package off. A package with a version before this one has **Roll back**. HiveHub's Packages tab shows what each package holds before it is downloaded.
- Hivemind moves the data on your machine to the shape a new version reads, once, when the app, a `hive` command or `hive host` first starts: each change is recorded in `<config>/hivemind/migrations/ledger.json`, the files it changes are copied first into `migrations/backups/`, and two processes starting at once wait for each other. `hive migrations` (`--json`) lists what ran, by which build, what is still to run and the last failure; `hive migrations restore <id>` puts the data back as it was before that change (refused with exit 7 while the app or `hive host` runs), and the next start makes it again. A `hive` command started while an older app still runs leaves the files that app reads where they are and says to quit it.
- A `settings.json` from before plugin state has its switches moved into `plugins.state` once, by a migration, instead of on every read; the old keys, the unread `migrated` flag, `settings.json.bak`, `agents-before-v2-0128/` and the `machines-migrated` marker go into `migrations/backups/`.
- **Settings → Plugins** shows a package that holds several plugins as one row with one switch; open it to see its members. A member can be switched off on its own, and on only while its package is on; each keeps its own settings page.
- Packages can come from HiveHub: `hive plugins install @owner/name` of a listed package installs every member at once, each file checked against the index, and **Settings → Plugins → HiveHub** lists packages with what each holds, where its code runs and what it asks for before you install. `hive plugins install --json` of a package is unchanged.
- Updating a package keeps it on unless something in it needs you to trust it again: a view or widget that asks for a new permission, or a tool or agent preset that is new or changed. `hive plugins install` lists each member that changed, `--dry-run` shows that list and installs nothing, and `hive plugins rollback <package>` puts back the version in use before, on or off as it was. `--json` adds `changes` and `installed`.
- A plugin that is part of a package changes only with it: `hive plugins install`, `remove` or `enable` of one plugin apart from its package is refused with exit 7 (`part_of_a_package`), naming the package. One can be switched off on its own, and on again only while its package is on. `hive plugins remove`, `enable` and `disable` take a package's id. A package's `"replaces": ["@owner/name"]` takes over a plugin of its owner installed on its own, keeping what you set it to.
- **Breaking:** a package can hold several plugins: a folder with a `hivemind-package.json` (`apiVersion: 2`) names a folder for each of its `tools`, `widgets` and `views`, and its agent presets in `agents`. `hive plugins install` checks every member as its kind is checked and installs them as one package; a member whose id is not the package owner's, or that another installed package holds, is refused. `hive plugins list` names the package of each plugin that shares one. `hive packages inspect` reads `apiVersion: 2` only and lists `members` in place of `views`.
- **Breaking:** every plugin you install is a package, kept in `<config>/hivemind/packages/`: each version's files in `store/`, never changed once installed, and `installed.json` saying which version is in use, whether it is on and what it holds. A folder of one plugin is a package of one; `hive plugins remove` removes its package, and an install that fails halfway leaves the version you had. `hive plugins install --json` gives the package (`id`, `name`, `version`, `digest`, `on`, `members` with each one's `kind`, `id`, `dir` and `permissions`, `replaced`) in place of `kind`, `dir` and `enabled`. Views, widgets and tools installed before this release are not read from `views/`, `widgets/` and `tools/`: install them again.
- Security: the hub kit refuses a plugin, or skips an index entry, whose name or description holds characters that can rewrite a terminal, whose files would land on one another (named twice in any letter case, or one inside another), or whose package members' folders overlap, or whose agent note or launch settings hold such characters; a value it quotes from a folder, and a manifest's own refusal, is escaped; and one entry nested too deep to read no longer empties the whole catalog.
- Settings ▸ Plugins ▸ HiveHub names each plugin it cannot show, one line each with why (a kind this Hivemind does not know, an entry it cannot read), under the ones it lists.
- The package format (`hivemind-package.json`, its member kinds) and package entries in the index live in the hub kit, so the app and every hub check a package with the same code: `checkPluginFolder` reads each member as its kind is read alone. The kit tarball depends on `zod` from npm.
- Each release carries the hub kit, `hivemind-hub-<version>.tgz`: the plugin index reader and the plugin checks, with nothing else to install, for a hub to depend on by the release's address.
- A plugin published from the root of its repository installs from HiveHub: its entry, whose folder is empty, was refused by the app.
- Before a plugin from HiveHub installs, the app and `hive` check the downloaded folder with the same checks a hub runs before listing it (`checkPluginFolder` in the hub kit): files, manifest, id and category. A folder that fails, or says it is another plugin, is not installed, and the install says why.
- The plugin catalog reads HiveHub's index through the new hub kit, `@hivemind/hub`: an entry this Hivemind cannot read is skipped with the reason, instead of the whole index being refused, and `hive views/widgets/tools/agents install <name>` says why when the one you asked for is skipped. An index may list 2,000 plugins (4 MB) instead of 500.
- Settings ▸ Plugins has a tab per kind, as HiveHub does: Agents, Views, Widgets and Tools, each with its count, in place of the kind menu; a tab lists only its kind, under its categories, and the page remembers the last tab.
- Security: only the person switches plugins on or off and changes their settings. `hive plugins enable|disable|set|reset`, choosing a service's `workspaces`, and `hive config set plugins.…` run from an agent's tile or with a program's token are refused (`PERSON_ONLY`, exit 6), saying what to ask the person to run. An agent could switch on a tool plugin it had just written.
- **Breaking:** every `hive` command's `--json` has one shape. A result is one JSON object on one line on stdout, without `{"ok":true,"data":…}` around it; a list goes under a name (`hive list --json` is `{"issues":[…]}`, `hive ctl frames` `{"frames":[…]}`, `list-workspaces` `{"workspaces":[…]}`). A failure is `{"error":{"code","message","fix"?}}` on stderr with its code in capitals, and always exits non-zero. A command written wrong (a missing argument, an unknown verb, an unknown flag such as `--timout`, which used to be ignored) is a usage error, exit 2, with `--json` too. `focus`, `connect` and `disconnect` say what they did. `--help` ends with the exit codes, from the same table the CLI exits with, and shows the whole command (`hive ctl spawn`).
- **Breaking:** an agent asleep is one answer for every verb that needs it running: `send`, `keys`, `read`, and `connect` at either end are refused `ASLEEP`, exit 9, with the fix `hive ctl wake <tile>`. `send` used to fail with FAILED (exit 1) and `read` returned `"finalStatus":"asleep"` (exit 7). `wake` on an agent awake already says so and exits 0, as `sleep` on one asleep already does. `sleep` with no target never puts the agent asking to sleep. A read after a wake waits for what the agent says next, never the reply from before its sleep. A worker's `report` to a starter asleep waits in its inbox and says so (`"asleep":true`, "delivered to <starter>'s inbox; it is asleep and reads it when woken"), and the starter is told once woken.
- When an agent or a program starts an agent, the window stays where you are; the new tile shows unread in Layers from its first change. Settings ▸ Plugins ▸ Agents, **When an agent starts another agent**, chooses **Stay where I am** (the default) or **Go to it** (`agents.whenAgentStartsAgent`). An agent you start from the keyboard, a menu, the Add dialog or a view is still shown. `tile.opened` says who opened the tile (`by`).
- New `hive guide agents` prints the guide for driving other agents, as the installed `hive` ships it: the same text `hive add skill` writes, so an agent in a tile has it whether or not a folder has the skill. Each agent's tile has `HIVEMIND_GUIDE="hive guide agents"` pointing to it. `hive guide work|browser|workflow` print the other guides.
- `hive ctl connect`'s help says what it does: src's finished replies go to dst's inbox, and one line typed into dst tells it, which starts a turn of its own. It used to say they go into dst's input.
- **Breaking:** `hive ctl agents --json` names each field as the flag that sets it: `name` is the tile's name (`spawn --name`, was `agent`), `agent` is the program it runs (`spawn --agent`, was `program.id`), and `workspace` is `{id, name}` (was `workspace` and `name`). An agent put to sleep shows `"state":"asleep"`. The text lines show the program too.
- New `hive ctl tiles [--json]` lists every tile of the workspace, browsers, tools and terminals with the agents: its id, kind, name and frame, an agent's state and program, and a tool's plugin.
- **Breaking:** `hive ctl read` tells the truth when no reply will come. An agent sent nothing since its last reply was read, and not working, answers at once `"finalStatus":"idle"` (exit 7) instead of waiting out its time and saying it is still working. `--poll` on a busy agent says `"working"`, never `"timeout"`. A tool, browser or shell tile is refused `NO_AGENT` (exit 5), "no agent runs in tile …". Workspace API 0.33 adds `idle`, `working` and `NO_AGENT` to `agent.read`.
- **Breaking:** a tile that is not open, closed or never opened, is not found (exit 5) for every verb that names one: `send`, `read`, `stream`, `rename`, `focus`, `keys`, `connect`, `disconnect`, `close`. It used to be FORBIDDEN (exit 6). An agent's or a program's token gets the same answer, word for word, for a tile of another workspace, so it cannot learn which tiles exist there; only the person's own devices are told a tile is another workspace's (FORBIDDEN). An approval request that is not waiting is not found too (exit 5, was 2).
- Attach photos to a message on the phone and browser page: pick or take one, paste or drop it. At most 4, each up to 5 MB (the page redraws one over 1 MB as a JPEG of at most 2048 px), checked by their first bytes, kept 7 days in a private folder of the tile's under the app's data (`~/.hivemind/photos` on a saved machine), and given to the agent as its path; codex, droid and opencode attach it as an image (`pastedImages`).
- A message you send from your phone or browser while Claude Code works now shows in its chat at once and stays there: Claude Code takes it into the running turn and records it differently from a prompt, and the chat skipped that record. A message held until the agent's turn ends (an agent that reads none mid-turn) shows as queued, with **Send now**, which stops the turn; held ones go in order. codex, pi, droid and kiro-cli now take a message mid-turn too, as Claude Code does. `agent.send` answers `held: true` for a held message (workspace API 0.33).
- The website's hero is recorded again with the new Island look, close on one island as a new agent starts there, and without the blank and white frames; each tab starts on the frame where its prompt is typed, because the recording is drawn one frame at a time and its step times come from the frame count. Phones get their own take in a phone-sized window, so no terminal line is cut off
- A view's `hivemind-view.json` can give a one-line `description` and an `.svg` `icon` from its folder; the view switcher, Settings ▸ Plugins and HiveHub's list show them, as they do for widgets and tools.
- Every README links its guide page, and the API SDK's package pages (npm `homepage`, PyPI project links) point at the guide.
- `hive ctl agents --session <id>` finds the agent in that session, as its hooks said it or as the app tracks its tile's session to resume it; none is exit 5. The CLI reference documents the `{seq, agents}` shape, and says `read` gives an agent's clean reply while `stream --snapshot` gives the raw lines its terminal printed.
- `hive ctl whoami [--json]` says which app it reaches (`hivemind` or `hivemind-dev`, from its data folder), the drive and control sockets, and who you are there: the person, an agent (its workspace folder, tile and session) or a program. `hive ctl list-workspaces` prints each workspace's prefix, title and folder, one a line. An agent or a program refused another workspace or tile is told the folder its token drives and that another needs the person or a grant (`hive grant create`); `--repo` help says so too.
- Agents: a worker whose session exits or restarts (a crash, a reboot, a restart in its tile) still reports to the agent that started it, and its parent's restart no longer cuts it off; only closing a tile forgets who started it. The `hivemind` skill tells agents to reach other tiles with `hive ctl send`, `report`, `inbox` and `read`.
- Views can tell an agent is asleep (view protocol 1.14, feature `sleep`): its tile in `structure` carries `asleep: { since }`, `hm.commands.wakeAgent(tileId)` and `sleepAgent(tileId)` wake it and put it to sleep (permission `workspace:spawn`), and `hm.prompt` to a sleeping agent is refused `ASLEEP`, as `hive ctl send` is.
- Settings ▸ Plugins lists what you have and what HiveHub has under categories, in the same order and with the same names as the Add menu; each HiveHub entry carries its manifest's category, and every agent is under Agents.
- A Browser tile's page is sharp at any canvas zoom: once the canvas stops moving, the page is redrawn at the zoom instead of stretched from 100%. The page's own zoom (Ctrl + / −) multiplies it and no longer carries to other tiles showing the same site.
- Quitting asks first while agents or terminals run here (⌘Q on a Mac, Ctrl+Q elsewhere, or closing the last window): **Keep them running**, **Stop everything** (as `hive daemon stop` does; each comes back when the app starts again) or **Cancel**, with **Don't ask again** and a matching choice in Settings → About and updates. **Quit and stop agents** in the Open Recent menu stops everything without asking.
- **Breaking:** every plugin you install is a package, kept in `<config>/hivemind/packages/`: each version's files in `store/`, never changed once installed, and `installed.json` saying which version is in use, whether it is on and what it holds. A folder of one plugin is a package of one; `hive plugins remove` removes its package, and an install that fails halfway leaves the version you had. `hive plugins install --json` gives the package (`id`, `name`, `version`, `digest`, `on`, `members` with each one's `kind`, `id`, `dir` and `permissions`, `replaced`) in place of `kind`, `dir` and `enabled`. Views, widgets and tools installed before this release are moved into packages on the first start, each on or off as it was and with its settings (a tool whose manifest says the old `owner/name` id is moved to `@owner/name`, its switch and settings with it); their old folders `views/`, `widgets/` and `tools/` are kept in `<config>/hivemind/migrations/backups/`, and one the checks of today refuse is named and left there.
- The website scrolls and plays smoothly on phones: the hero's step bar and its opening lines no longer keep the page busy every frame, the views stack is flat on small screens and dims its back cards without a filter, and links on phones are easier to tap.
- The website's hero tabs (a view, a widget, a tool) jump to their part of the recording again, and the video streams in pieces instead of all at once; the hero says it in one line with two buttons, and the download section is one install box that switches between Linux, macOS and Windows.
- The website's hero opens on an agent's terminal at full size where you ask for a view, then a widget and a tool, in one take; its first frame paints at once as a small image, the recording follows when the page is idle, phones get their own crop, and the site's fonts are served from the site itself.
- The website can be found by search and answer engines: `robots.txt` and a `sitemap.xml` of every page (site and guide), a canonical address on each page (`/guide/x` and `/x/index.html` now send you on to `/guide/x/` and `/x/`), structured data (the app, the site, the FAQ, and each guide page with its breadcrumb), full share cards on guide pages, and `llms.txt` and `llms-full.txt` served at the root. The home page's title, lede and questions say what Hivemind is and name the agents it runs. The guide's Markdown and `llms.txt` links no longer point at `/guide/guide/…`.
- The guide's **How it works** tab explains the system: an architecture overview of what runs where, a page for each part (the desktop app, the background process, the `hive` command, plugins, hive-net, the phone page, machines and hosts) and the core ideas, with diagrams drawn inline in the page's light or dark theme, each followed by its facts as text for the Markdown copy and `llms.txt`.
- Widgets: `hive.reducedMotion` and `hive.on("motion")` tell a widget's code that the person asked for less motion, so a widget that moves its card can hold still.
- The website and README say Hivemind runs every coding agent, not a count of them; the agent grid ends with "Any other", which your agent adds in one file.
- The website's home page leads with what only Hivemind does: its hero is a recording of the real app, sharp on retina screens, where an agent switches the workspace to the Island view, then adds a CI light widget and the Flaky tests tool; the README lists your own views, tools and widgets and the agent-driven workspace first.
- The guide at hivemind.griiken.com/guide has tabs, search, light and dark, a copy-ready prompt on each page, **Copy page** and a menu to open a page in ChatGPT, Claude or Cursor; every page is also Markdown at `/guide/<page>.md`, and all of them are in `/guide/llms.txt` and `/guide/llms-full.txt`.
- API SDKs: `hivemind-api` for TypeScript (Node and Bun) and Python (3.9+, standard library only) connects a program to a workspace with a token from `hive grant create`, calls any method, hears events, and lists, starts, reads and answers agents without hand-writing the drive socket protocol. Each release carries them (`hivemind-api-<version>.tgz`, `hivemind_api-<version>-py3-none-any.whl`), and the installers put them in `sdk/` beside `hive`: `npm install ~/.hivemind-app/sdk/hivemind-api.tgz`, `pip install ~/.hivemind-app/sdk/python`. `hive` now drives the socket through the TypeScript one.

- API access: `agent.transcript` (Workspace API 0.32) tells a program on this machine which session an agent is in and where its session file is, so it can follow the agent's own records. A phone, a guest, or an agent on another machine is pointed to `agent.conversation` instead.

- API access: `agent.approval` (Workspace API 0.31) and `hive ctl approval <request>` show what a supervised worker's approval request would do, with its tool's input in full (an edit's text, not only its file), so a program or a person answering it sees what it allows.

- Claude: an agent stopped at startup on the "Settings Warning" (a settings rule was skipped) or the one-time Bypass Permissions mode warning now shows as waiting on you, saying which screen it is (`settings-warning`, `bypass`), to programs, the phone and notifications alike. The bypass warning is answered only at its terminal. Comes with the Claude plugin's update.

- Agents: the limit of 16 starts a minute now counts each program apart (you, each API token, each agent you started, each with the agents started under it), under 64 a minute for the machine, so one program starting agents in a loop no longer stops you or other programs starting them; a start refused for another reason no longer counts.

- API access: a program that catches up with `agent.since` after the app or `hive host` restarted is told to list again, instead of being handed the new process's changes as if they followed its own.

- API access: a program that stops reading its connection is disconnected once 16 MiB wait for it, instead of growing the app's memory; one that never says hello is disconnected after 10 s; `agent.read` given more than about 24 days waits instead of answering at once; and a plugin service's log folder deleted while it runs no longer stops the app.

- API access: with `hive host` running, tokens made and taken back in the app's Settings now work there at once; before, a new one was refused and one taken back kept working. A plugin service's token left behind by an app that was killed stops working as soon as the app or host starts again.

- API access: a token made to last 25 days or more (`hive grant create --expires 30d`) no longer has its connection closed right after it opens.

- A supervised agent's tool use waits on its starter again: `hive ctl approve` and the drive API's `agent.answer` find it in the agent list, waiting on approval, until it is answered. The machine's terminal daemon keeps each session's status, and it overwrote the wait the app held.

- An agent's issue brief quotes a review comment as it really arrives, and the server set-up brief checks enrolment where `hive network use` prints it.

- The example widgets, tools and views are on HiveHub, published from dip497/hivemind-plugins; install one with `hive widgets install @dip497/<name>`, `hive tools install dip497/<name>` or `hive views install @dip497/<name>`, or from Settings ▸ Plugins. The Agent board view is `@dip497/agent-board`, and a new widget, Dog, barks when an agent waits on you.

- A widget with no card reaches the right edge of the screen when it moves there; it stopped as far short as a card is wider than it.

- Settings → Servers: on a fresh install, "Set up my server with an agent" is the first thing on the page; the page shows and checks a network an agent switched to with `hive network use`, without a refresh.

- A new agent starts as the agent you chose in Settings everywhere one starts (Send to agent, a view, a plugin, the toolbar), or the first one installed, never one this machine does not have. Send to agent offers a new tile of every installed agent, your chosen one first, its search finds them, and a prompt sent to an agent tile reaches it whichever agent it runs.

- The guide is rewritten, from `guide/`: Get started, Use Hivemind, Concepts, Extend, API, Reference and Help, with a first-agent tutorial that ends with you answering the agent, screenshots of the real app, and new pages for keyboard shortcuts, troubleshooting by symptom, the FAQ, a glossary, install, update and uninstall. Every page starts with a prompt to paste into your coding agent.

- Agents keep running through an upgrade on every platform: macOS no longer refuses an upgrade while agents run, Windows keeps each version in a folder of its own, an AppImage started directly keeps its agents when the app quits, and a machine's agents survive an update of `hive` there.

- Agents keep running through an app upgrade: the new app takes over the running terminal daemon, and a tile shows what its agent did meanwhile. When a release changes how the daemon talks, agents finish their turn, then come back in the same conversation.

- **Breaking:** `hive daemon status --json` reports `protocol` instead of `buildStamp`.

- An agent's notification card is drawn over widgets instead of behind them; dialogs now open over Settings and a fullscreen tile, and tooltips over dialogs.

- Layers: "Hide asleep" in the View menu leaves asleep agents out of the list; a line at the end says how many are hidden and shows them again when clicked.

- New widgets stack down the right edge by their real height, so a tall widget is no longer covered by the ones added after it; a column that reaches the bottom goes on to its left.

- A guest who joins a workspace sees its board: when the host's last change was not to its frames and tiles (a tile moved, say), the guest's board stayed empty though connected.

- An agent started with `hive ctl spawn` no longer draws garbled text: the tile's size, given while the session was still starting (seconds on a remote machine), was lost, so the agent kept drawing at 120×36 inside a tile of another size.

- The website's home page is redesigned around agents building the workspace: the 21 agents on HiveHub, widgets, tools and views, the Queue view, machines over ssh, the phone page and a shared workspace, then what stays yours, measured numbers with their load, and the download. It also shows screenshots of twenty example plugins. Each clip slot shows a real screenshot until its recording lands.

- The website's home page opens on a video recorded from the real app: a Claude Code agent writes a widget for its issue, installs it and ticks off the steps as it works. 
- The guide's diagrams are drawn in the site's own colours and fonts; the issue-states diagram now says nothing enforces the order, and the terminal-size and widget diagrams match what the code does.

- The website moved from `docs/` to `site/`; `docs/` keeps the engineering notes, and a change there no longer redeploys the site.

- `hive agents install --help` and the installer name amp, an agent on HiveHub, as the example instead of gemini.

- `hive join` refused from an agent's tile, a program or another device now says what works: the person runs `hive join <link>` in a terminal of their own, and their own devices need no link (Open recent → On your devices). Who may join is unchanged.
- A workspace named by a folder name two workspaces share is refused by `hive share`, `hive people`, `hive grant create` and `hive ctl --repo` alike, listing each with its id or issue prefix and folder, instead of picking one; each of them now takes a folder, a name, an id or an issue prefix (`hive workspace list`), and a registered folder the app does not serve counts too.
- With the app running, `hive host add <folder>` and `hive share <folder>` have the app serve that folder, with no window opening, instead of saying `hive host` is not running; `hive share` serves a folder it is not serving yet before making the link. Only the person may: from an agent's tile it is refused.
- `hive host install` and `hive host run` refuse once while the app runs here and name the commands that work; the service no longer restarts into the same refusal.
- An agent started on a machine that has only `hive` (one added over ssh, a `hive host`) now reports its turns and shows in `hive ctl agents`: the start installs the agent there from your app's, where the machine has none of its own. `hive ctl --repo` takes a workspace on a machine by its address (`ssh://…`, `machine://…`).
- `hive ctl frame add --folder <path> [--machine <name or id>] [--name <frame>]` opens a frame on a folder of a saved machine or another of your devices, as **Open folder here…** does, so an agent can set up a remote builder alone. Workspace API 0.30: `frame.open` takes `machine`.
- An agent in a frame on your other device that runs the app now reports back: its turn end reaches `hive ctl read`, the starter's `hive ctl inbox` and its tile's status, as on your own computer. Every machine's terminal daemon now holds the agents' control-plane socket and passes what they report to each device watching, and the app on that machine shows each session's status as its daemon keeps it, so a tile no longer reads done while its agent works or stays on starting after its turn.
- A widget's `hive.d.ts` now types issues: `onIssues` and `issues.read` say each field, and an issue's `assignee` is `{ type, id }`, so a widget that printed it as text showed `[object Object]` and now fails its own type check instead.

- A widget can show a long line whole: a `text` node with `wrap: true` goes on to the next line instead of being cut short with …, as every widget line was, a title too.

### Added

- Twenty plugins to try in `examples/`: widgets (Waiting on you, Agent pulse, Focus timer, CI light, Test meter, Spend today, Issue burndown, Launchpad), tools (dependency audit, flaky tests, changelog writer, release checklist, bundle size, SQLite browser, log tail, API explorer) and views (a 3D Constellation of your agents, Timeline, Agents by state, Workspace map), plus four wallpapers in `examples/wallpapers`.
- A widget's `hive.hm.capabilities`, `features` and `settings` are an array or an object from its first line of code, empty until the app sends them, instead of a function that threw on `.includes()`; `hive.on("hm")` fires when they arrive or change.

- A `tint` a widget's code draws colours its card, on the board and floating, as a sticky note's colour does.

- `hive ctl plugin close` takes the plugin as `plugin open` names it (`widget:@owner/name`, `tool:owner/name`, `view:id`) when one copy is open, as well as its instance.

- `hive ctl plugin close` takes any widget off the board by its id, a built-in one drawn without code (Checklist, Sticky note, Text) too.
- `hive ctl open-tool` takes a tool package's id when the package has one tool, and a package with several says their full ids; `hive tools list` and `hive tools install` print each tool's full id. Installing a tool again with the same code byte for byte keeps it on; changed code still goes off, and the install says which.
- A tool plugin can show a server on this machine in a frame (`http://127.0.0.1:*`, `http://localhost:*`); other addresses stay blocked. When the app blocks something a tool loads, or its code throws an error nothing catches, its tile stops and shows why, and `hive ctl plugin list` reads it as failed with that reason.
- A page shown in a tool's frame cannot move the app's window to another page, and any page other than the app's own gets no access to the app. Tools get `Frame` from `hm-plugin://host/sdk.js`, a sandboxed frame for a server on this machine.
- `hive ctl tile look <tile> [--out f.png]` (Workspace API `tile.look`) brings a tile into view, waits until its tool has drawn, and saves a picture of it, with the tool's state and the last 100 lines its code wrote to the console. `hive ctl plugin list` says whether each plugin has drawn yet (`drew`).
- `hive ctl tile look` raises the tile above the others and refuses with `OCCLUDED` (exit 7), naming what is drawn over it (a pinned tile, a dialog, a notification), instead of picturing it with the tile.
- `hive ctl open-tool <tool> --state <json>` opens a tool's tile with its first saved state (what the tool's `useTileState` reads). A tool's tile shows the normal pointer instead of the canvas's grab cursor where the tool sets none. The tool guide covers tool ids and `open-tool`, framing a local server, what `host.run` does (the login shell's `PATH`, no shell, 60 s, 10 MB), looking at a tile, reinstalling, and what the canvas does with the pointer and keys over a tile; `hive tools new` prints its address.
- A tool whose code does not load is a failed tile `hive ctl plugin list` shows with why, `plugin open` exits 1 for, and `plugin close` closes; a one-of-a-kind tool (Issues, Code, or `"singleton": true`) opened again by `hive ctl open-tool` brings its tile forward instead of opening another.
- **Breaking:** one command for every plugin you write or add: `hive plugins new tool|widget|view <name> [--dir]` (prints the steps to see it), `hive plugins install <folder|id>` (the kind read from the folder or HiveHub; `owner/name` and `@owner/name` are always HiveHub's, so a folder is written as a path, `./x`), `hive plugins remove`, `hive plugins list [--kind]`. `hive tools`, `hive widgets` and `hive views` are gone. A plugin is named by its id alone; `<kind>:<id>` only where two kinds share one.
- **Breaking:** a tool plugin is `@owner/name`, as widgets and views are (`@hivemind/web`, `@hivemind/issues/issues`); `owner/name` is no longer read. `hive ctl plugin open` takes a plugin by its id alone, with `tool:`, `widget:` or `view:` only where two kinds share one.
- A new tool starter carries its types: the tile SDK, the `hm` client and the app's host modules, with a `jsconfig.json` that checks `index.js` (`tsc -p jsconfig.json`). `hive plugins api --kind tool|widget|view` prints what a plugin's code is given, as declared in this version.
- A widget whose code draws a node the app has no drawing for (`{ type: "chart" }`), or throws, is failed in `hive ctl plugin list` with why (`children[1] is a "chart", which the app does not draw; it draws: …`), instead of an empty card that says ready.
- A new plugin's PROMPT.md is plain Markdown for the agent (the guide without its page components, pictures or the prompt meant for pasting, links made whole), and its loop checks the code against its types first; for a tool it ends with `hive ctl tile look`.
- `hive plugins check <folder> [--json]` checks a tool, widget or view in three steps, naming the file and line of each problem: its types (its own `tsconfig.json` or `jsconfig.json`), its manifest, and its code loaded and drawn once against a fake app (each tile of a tool, a widget's first drawing, a view built and its page loaded). `hive plugins new` prints it as the step before installing, and PROMPT.md's loop starts with it. A new view builds with a source map, so a problem points into `src/`. The draw runs the plugin's own code, as `bun test` would: in a `hive` of its own, in the plugin's folder, with only `PATH` and `HOME` of your environment; a widget runs in a worker with nothing of the machine, and the type check always uses the CLI's own TypeScript and the kind's own settings and type files: it never reads the plugin's tsconfig.json or jsconfig.json, and refuses a code file that links outside the plugin's folder.

## [2026.10.3] — 2026-10-18

### Added

- Tool plugins install from HiveHub: Settings ▸ Plugins lists them under Tools, and `hive tools install owner/name` takes one by its id (a folder of that name is still the folder). Every file is checked against the index, its `hivemind-tool.json` must say the id it was listed under, and it arrives switched off, an update too.

## [2026.10.2] — 2026-10-18

### Added

- An agent manifest's `turn.ended` entry can name the payload field that holds the turn's reply (`reply: last_assistant_message`): the generic hook hands it over before the turn end, with `hive-net` natively, so an agent whose payload carries its reply needs no script of its own for `hive ctl read`. A reply longer than 256 KB is cut to its start.
- Agents drive the plugins in the app as one lifecycle: `hive ctl plugin list` shows every tool tile, widget with code and view running, each starting, ready or failed (with why), and the views there are; `hive ctl plugin open` answers once what it opened can be called, so a `call` right after it works; a call to a plugin still starting waits for it; `hive ctl plugin close` closes a tool's tile, takes a widget off the board or leaves a view. Workspace API 0.27 (`plugin.list`, `plugin.close`).
- The hivemind skill covers every `hive ctl` verb (sessions and `spawn --resume`, rename, tools, plugin commands, view events) and points to networks, sharing, grants, machines, hosts and push. The hive skills now follow the app: starting an agent in a workspace brings the skills it has up to this version, and `hive add skill` replaces older copies, edits too; keep a skill of your own under another name.
- Remove a workspace from this computer from the Layers panel's title menu: hover it and press ×, or press Delete. Its board here goes; its folder and files stay. A workspace with a terminal still running says so and stays.
- The Android app's first tab is Home, as on the phone page: what needs you on top, then what is working, the pinned, and each project's frames and tiles as your computer's Layers panel lists them, each with what it last said. A search and machine chips filter it. It replaces the Needs you and Agents tabs.
- The full app in a browser tab: Settings → Advanced → In a browser serves it on 127.0.0.1 behind a key that changes each launch. For another device, put it behind `tailscale serve`.
- Connect: a computer that uses this one's offer to connect (its link, or its six words found nearby) is asked about first. A prompt shows its name and three words that its own screen shows too, with Deny, Someone else (let into this window's workspace, as Can view, Can edit board or Can use terminals) and It's me — the words match. It's me makes it yours only after the person there says the words match as well. Open recent → Join takes an offer's link or words, shows the same three words, and asks before this computer becomes someone else's. A request nobody answers within three minutes is no.
- The phone page and `hive-phone connect <link> [--sure yes|no]` use a computer's offer to connect: they show the same three words, wait for the person there, and keep the phone as the person's only once the person at the phone says the words match.
- The iOS and Android apps connect with a computer's offer too: scanned or pasted, they show the three words, and the phone becomes the person's only after a yes on each side. They point to Settings → Connect.
- Each machine shows what kind of computer it is (laptop, desktop, server, virtual machine or container), as it reports when checked, and so does This computer. `hive machine add --json` includes it as `kind`.
- `hive ctl inbox [--wait 90s]`: what other agents sent an agent (a worker's reply or report, that it waits on an answer, an approval it asks), each given once. `hive ctl send --interrupt` stops a worker's turn before sending.
- Home on the phone (Android and the phone page) looks like your computer's Layers panel: each agent's own logo in a round mark, a folder or branch per frame in its colour, fold arrows with counts, a dot on what you have not seen, and the same View menu (Status, Activity, Group by, Sort by). Choices and folds are kept on the phone.

### Changed

- `hive-phone unpair` on a network with a push server registers there again before it tells the computer, which may take the phone off the network as it forgets it and so had the push server refuse; and it no longer hides a failure there: `--json` adds `"stillPushes"` (and `"pushError"` when true), and the command exits 2 while the unpaired computer can still push to the phone.
- One terminal printing a flood no longer holds up typing in the others. The terminal daemon reads and redraws every session's screen on a thread of its own, and a session whose screen falls 256 K characters behind waits for it alone. Under a 10 MB flood with its screen asked for every half second, another window's key echoes in 3–7 ms, with the slowest at 0.3 s (load 22–50 on 16 CPUs); before, the median was 162 ms and the slowest 4 s (load 49), and the same flood did not finish in 15 minutes. Showing 30 long terminals at once no longer freezes the daemon for 2.3–2.7 s: its longest pause is 11–39 ms (load 12–16).
- A guest's edits after one the host refuses no longer vanish: the refused change is undone on every copy, and what the guest does next reaches the host.
- A guest on a slow link is sent nothing while the link has no room; when room returns, one update carries everything changed meanwhile.
- A device that stops reading no longer grows your computer's memory or slows the others. What your computer sends a device waits for room on its link; one that falls 16 MB behind is disconnected as too slow. A stalled device held 120 MB in `hive-net` after 96 MB sent to it; now 26 MB, with the link closed at 17 MB (load 4 on 16 CPUs), and another device's echo stays at 1 ms. A change sent to 50 devices costs the app 0.73 ms per 4 KB of terminal output instead of 2.2 ms.
- A window that takes another window's change no longer sends its layout back: what it would save equals what it read, so nothing is sent.
- A new guest of a shared workspace is sent the workspace as it is, not every change since its owner's app started: 487 KB for a 5000-object board, however long the app has run. A guest that cannot take another guest's offline change asks for the workspace again and keeps its own edits.
- A guest's edit is checked on a copy of the workspace kept up to date, not on a fresh copy of the whole document each time: a guest's typed character takes about 9 ms on the host at 5000 board objects instead of 600 ms.
- **Breaking:** Workspace API 0.28. A board edit costs what it changed: a window sends only the objects it changed, each with the one it changed it from (`store.editObjects`, which replaces `store.setObjects`), `store.changed` names the board's objects that changed, and the other windows read those alone (`store.objectsWithIds`). A typed character in a 5000-object board takes about 15 ms instead of 2 s, and another window's read of it under 1 ms instead of 360 ms.
- Put agents to sleep with `hive ctl sleep [tile or frame] [--force]` and bring them back with `hive ctl wake`. A sleeping agent's process ends and frees its memory (five agents freed 2.1-2.7 GB); its tile keeps its name, and waking it continues the same conversation, on this computer, an ssh machine or another of your devices. A busy agent stays awake unless `--force`. `--json` lists what slept or woke and what was skipped with why, and the exit code is 7 when nothing was done. Messages sent to a sleeping agent wait until it is at its prompt again. Every agent that resumes a conversation wakes into its own, not the newest in its folder; a sleep that could not keep it is refused with why. `hive ctl read` of a sleeping agent answers at once, `finalStatus: "asleep"`, exit 7. Workspace API 0.29 adds `agent.sleep`, `agent.wake` and `terminal.saved`; `terminal.open` can answer `asleep` and `terminal.exit` says `slept`.
- Put a tile, a frame or every agent to sleep, or resume them, from Layers (a sleeping agent's row shows a moon) and from a frame's menu. A sleeping agent's tile is a black screen with Resume. An agent that is working or waiting on you is only put to sleep after you confirm.
- Saving a workspace after an edit costs what the edit changed: the change is added beside the saved board (`<workspace>.loro.updates`) and folded into it when the workspace goes quiet, instead of the whole board written again. A typed character in a 5000-object board takes 0.9 ms on disk instead of 600 ms.
- An agent's event costs the app about 0.23 ms instead of 1.9 ms, the same with 72 or 600 agents (load about 100 on 16 CPUs): the phones to tell are read from disk once, not on every turn's end, and nothing per event reads a file or looks through every workspace.
- An agent's hook passes on only the ids the app reads from what the agent tells it, never a tool's output: with `hive-net`, the terminal service spends about 2 ms on a hook whether the tool printed 100 bytes or 4 MB, instead of 24 ms at 1 MB and 74 ms at 4 MB (load about 70 on 16 CPUs, where accepting one connection alone costs 1 ms).
- What a waiting agent asks is read from its session file only after the agent wrote to it, and the file is looked for among Claude Code's project folders only until found: 0.2 to 0.4 ms per waiting agent instead of 19 to 23 ms (load about 70 on 16 CPUs).
- A session id an agent's hook reports is taken only when it is an id: one that reads as a flag or a path is not recorded, and is never put on the command line that resumes the tile. Each event no longer reads the tile's session file; it is written only when the session changes.
- The list of agents your phone follows is built in time that grows with the number of agents, not its square: 10 ms for 600 agents instead of 1.6 s (load about 80 on 16 CPUs). The workspaces each agent event looks at are kept until one of them changes.
- Opening the app or a window no longer sends every terminal's 5000 lines of history. Each terminal gets its newest 1000 lines and fetches the rest when you scroll to the top, keeping the line you were reading in place. With 30 terminals that is 4.6M characters instead of 22M, and about a quarter of the terminal daemon's time.
- Coming back to a terminal you scrolled past or switched away from no longer asks for its whole screen. It is sent only what it missed, up to 256 KB; it used to be sent all 5000 lines, which held up every terminal while the screen was rebuilt.
- An edit from someone you share a workspace with is announced only for what it changed. Each one used to announce the layout, the board and every view, so every window read all of them again and saved them back.
- Opening a tool call on your phone no longer freezes the app on a long session: it is looked for only in the part of the conversation you were shown. It used to read the session file back to its start, 4.6 s for a 400 MB file. Home on the phone also stops working things out once no phone follows it.
- Typing in a note on a busy board is faster: each edit wrote every object on the board and read it four times. It now reads it once and writes only what changed, 96 → 21 ms per character with 50 objects, 381 → 192 ms with 800.
- A change from another window, your phone or an agent no longer redraws every tile in the window. Only the tiles it changed are drawn again: with 100 tiles open, a rename used to rebuild all 100.
- Opening several agents at once no longer freezes the window for most of a second. Each terminal's GPU drawing is set up in a frame of its own, the focused one first, instead of all of them in one go.
- Terminals no longer freeze every two seconds while an agent prints. The saved copy of a busy terminal (what comes back after a crash) keeps its last 500 lines instead of all 5000, which took up to half a second to write and held every terminal's output meanwhile; quitting, closing a window and a program ending still save all of it.
- Agents changing status no longer slow every window down. The app used to re-read every workspace from disk several times per status change, 16 ms with 6 workspaces and 47 ms with 50; it is now under 1.1 ms.
- Phones open long conversations and terminal histories faster: the computer sends what is over 16 KB deflated, a third of the bytes or less, to a phone that says it reads it. Older phones and computers keep working with each other as before. Workspace API 0.27.
- Reopening the app no longer makes the terminal daemon rebuild every terminal's saved screen: it hands back the one it already has, so typing into terminals is not held up while the app starts.
- Canvas: panning no longer restyles and re-rasters every tile at the start and end of a pan, and the end of a pan re-renders nothing: about half the main-thread work per wheel notch on a large board.
- Startup: a window no longer starts eight diff-highlighting workers or loads the diff library before a diff is shown (2 MB less JavaScript to start), its first screen's fonts load before it is drawn, and it opens without waiting for the terminal daemon's build check.
- Phone page and Android app: one new look, written down in docs/design/phone-look-2026-10-15.md — neutral greys in light and dark, colour only for what waits on you, fails or changes; quiet rows with a trailing status mark; needs-you cards with the command and pill answers; a raised message box whose send becomes Stop; an All machine chip.
- iOS app: the same look as the phone page and Android: quiet tabs and rows with a small state mark, waits as cards with Allow over Deny, a message box whose send becomes Stop, chips on Start, numbered diff lines, reachable dots on Devices.
- iOS app: when the phone's lock cannot be asked for a reason other than you cancelling it, the app says why instead of doing nothing.
- Android app: the chat reads like a chat. The agent's text is 16/24 with clearer headings and room between turns; code blocks have a Copy button and bordered tables; held down, a message is copied. Tools used one after another fold into one row ("Read 3 · Edit 2 · Run 1 · 1 failed") that opens to them, and "working · 12s" shows under the chat while the agent works, with a spinner beside its name.
- Android app: smoother while agents work. The terminal behind a chat is not redrawn until it is opened; a session is resized once the keyboard settles, not on every frame it slides; a status line rewritten in place no longer relays out the terminal; markdown is read off the main thread, and a tool's result redraws its own row only. The phone's core reads its paired devices once each time they change, not on every update.
- Android app: the message box grows to six lines, and a question or a request for a reply is answered from it: one place to type. Start takes up to four photos with the prompt, kept on the computer the agent runs on, as the phone page does.
- Android app: Home is the app, with a gear opening Settings (devices, pairing, views). An agent opens on its chat, newest at the bottom, its wait card last. Tool rows are one line and open the full use on its own screen. The terminal opens from the header, fitted to the phone (Fit toggle). A diff scrolls sideways inside itself.
- iOS app: Home is the app, with no tabs; a gear opens Settings (devices, pairing, views). An agent opens on its chat, newest at the bottom with what it waits on last; tool rows are one line and open the use on its own screen; the terminal opens from the header, fitted to the phone, with Fit to let go.
- Connecting a phone: a phone that asks the network again with an offer it was already let in by, its first answer lost, gets in instead of "the voucher was used up".
- **Breaking:** the `tile.opened` event no longer carries the tile's `prompt`: the host starts the tile with it, and no client is told what it was asked to do.
- A machine is paused, not turned off: Pause stops this app connecting to it, and nothing on the machine stops. A machine that is away is grey, not red; one that needs a sign-in says Sign in, and pressing it opens the sign-in.
- In the Layers panel, an agent's working or unread dot sits on the corner of its icon, as a machine's does, instead of after its name or at the row's end.

- Text, corners and control heights come from one scale: seven named text sizes (20, 15, 14, 13, 12, 11, 10) in place of seventeen, buttons beside inputs in Settings are both 32px, shadows, scrims and the editor's selection follow the theme, and section headings are sentence case.
- Every choice from a list is the app's own select: the same height, border and corners as the field beside it, its list drawn like a menu, with keyboard and type-ahead; no operating-system blue or orange anywhere. Keyboard focus is one ring for the whole app, in the selection colour rather than the accent (so never amber), and checkboxes, sliders and scrollbars follow the theme.
- Settings is regrouped by what you do: General, Connect (Connect, API access), Plugins, Advanced (In a browser, Servers, About and updates), each page with a line saying what it is for. A browser that pairs is named by what it is ("Chrome on Android"). The update check is switched on About and updates only. Links to the old page names still open the new pages. API access is marked with a key, for its tokens.
- Settings → Plugins is one list: Installed or HiveHub, a search and a kind in one row, then a group per kind with its count. Every plugin's row looks the same and says its state in one badge (Default, In use, Off, Not installed, Installed); making one the default, using a view, switching one off and removing one are on its own page. Adding agents found on this machine sits under the agents. The refresh button is gone: the list is read again when the window comes back.
- Settings → Plugins: an installed agent whose program is missing says Needs setup, not Not installed; a row whose description only repeats its name shows the name alone; the HiveHub list ends with one line on checksums.
- Settings → Plugins reads as a list, not a table: no box or rule around the rows, and each row says what HiveHub says the plugin does instead of "Runs the claude command" or "Comes with the app". Making your own and adding one from a folder are one **Add** menu at the top. A plugin's page shows its own icon and one header, with its switch (and Make default or Use this view) beside it on every tab; agents installed from HiveHub say so. Remove is a quiet button until you point at it.
- Settings → Connect replaces People and Your devices: one Connect button shows a code for another computer, a phone or someone else, and you say who it is when it is used; requests waiting show on the page too. Have a code? Join enters one from elsewhere, also from Home. Below, who is connected: your devices with Unpair, and, for a workspace of yours, the people in it with their roles and where it runs, with Keep it running. QR codes fill their box at whole pixels a module with a four-module white margin and open full size when pressed; Esc closes.
- **Breaking:** messages between agents wait in the receiving agent's inbox; its terminal is only told `[hive] Your agents sent you messages. Read them: hive ctl inbox`, at its prompt. Nothing an agent wrote is typed into another's terminal. A reply taken with `hive ctl read` is not also delivered. Workspace API 0.26.
- **Breaking:** `hive ctl read` returns `finalStatus` `waiting` (exit 8) as soon as the worker stops on a question or a permission, with what it asks, instead of timing out.
- A worker that waits on a question is reported as a question, with its options, even when its agent's hook calls it a permission.
- **Breaking:** `hive host pair <code>` is gone, with the pairing code the app showed: run `hive host pair` on the host and enter the words it shows under Join on your computer.

### Security

- **Breaking:** an agent is heard on the control-plane socket only once it signs in with its tile's token, and only about its own tile: an event, a reply, a screen reading or a screen line sent unsigned or about another tile is dropped or refused, on the desktop, `hive host` and a remote machine's daemon, so one agent cannot change another's status or put words in its `hive ctl read`. A plugin's script that sends `agent.event` must call `initialize` first (as a notification, when it reads nothing back); the SDK's `emit` does. A manifest whose hook entry reports (`emit:`) needs `launch.hcp: true`.
- An agent manifest, from HiveHub or anywhere, can no longer change what every object in the app holds through a field path such as `__proto__.x` in its session mapping: a field path reads and writes a record's own fields only.
- A computer, phone or browser no longer becomes yours by entering a pairing code first: today's pairing gave the person key to whoever proved the code first, asking nobody. Connect asks you, shows three words on both screens, and the device is yours only after a yes on each side.

### Fixed

- `hive ctl spawn` run inside a folder set up for issues but never opened in the app no longer starts its agent there, on a board no window shows: listing that folder in the Layers panel's Projects no longer opens it as a workspace.
- A guest with two windows on the same shared terminal: hiding it in one window no longer stops it in the other, closing it in one no longer lets the host drop it while the other shows it, and asking for more history in one no longer resets the other's screen. Each window now has a connection of its own to the host for its terminals; everything else still comes once per computer. The app and `hive-net` speak daemon protocol 3; install them from one release, as before.
- A guest's change that its role does not allow can no longer reach the host by being sent before a change it waits on.
- A guest who may edit a shared workspace can no longer change whose it is: the owner's host refuses that change.
- A guest who edited a shared workspace while away, while its owner's app restarted, no longer parts from it for good: on reconnecting it takes the owner's board and sends its own edits on top, instead of the owner dropping every change it sends from then on.
- The phone no longer fails to save, or silently loses, what your computers last told it when two of them answer at once, or when you unpair one while it is connected.
- `hive ctl plugin call` and `open` exit with the code their failure has: 5 when the plugin is not running or has no such command, 4 when it did not answer in time, 2 when it runs twice and no `--tile` says which; before, every one exited 1. A slow command now ends with the plugin's own reason, not the CLI's timeout, which used to come first.
- A Claude agent whose folder-trust screen was answered now receives the messages sent to it; before, every one was held for a turn that never ended.
- A path holding a long run of slashes is read at once; one with 50,000 of them took ten seconds.
- Connect's Phone browser tab, on a network whose server does not serve the phone page, and Reach devices elsewhere → Serve from one of my devices offer **Set it up with an agent**: pick an agent installed here, and it asks you how your devices should reach this computer (Tailscale, your own domain, a tunnel, a forwarded port), sets up the server with the phone page and puts this computer on it, asking you for anything only you can do. The command shown there before had no `--phone`, so a server set up from it never gave a phone link.
- The Machines pane's status dot sits clear of the machine's icon instead of covering its corner.
- Phones draw an agent's mark in the colours its manifest gives, as your computer does, instead of all in the text colour.
- Settings → Connect has two tabs, Hivemind app and Phone browser, each with its own QR code and Copy the link. Phone browser says so when your network has no server serving the page.
- Layers panel, Machines: a machine's refresh, + and ⋯ buttons no longer cover its status words on hover.
- Phone page and Android app: the View menu says "Last activity" as the computer's does, and has Reset to defaults; a shell's row shows a terminal mark, not a window.
- The workspace menu in the Layers panel lists every workspace this computer holds, as your phone's Home does, and every folder set up for issues that you have not opened yet. Folders whose issues share a prefix, and folders with no issues, were missing from it.
- Android app: scrolling the terminal of a program that draws its own screen (Claude Code, less, vim) scrolls the program, as the phone page and your computer do. It scrolled nothing.
- A terminal fitted to a phone says so on your computer, with Use this window's size to take it back. The phone's Fit turns off once the computer takes the size, and the phone gives the size back when the app or page leaves the screen.
- A question Claude asks still shows on your phone with its options after an hour. Claude's hook gives up waiting after an hour, and the phone used to fall back to a bare "Permission requested".
- The Android and iOS apps answer an agent's questions as the phone page does: each question with its options as buttons, several answered together, each in its place, and a permission card shows its tool and command. Before, a question took only a line of text.
- The app served to a browser opens in Safari, on an iPhone too; before, it showed a black screen.
- A canvas moved by one wheel notch is where it was left after a restart; before, it reopened where the last longer pan had left it.
- Recents lists a folder's older sessions, and listing them no longer stalls the app on a long agent history.
- Opening a tool use in an agent's conversation on your phone is instant on long sessions.
- HiveHub and a listed plugin's page open at once after the first fetch; a listed plugin's page no longer says the plugin is gone while loading.
- A line typed or pasted into an agent's terminal with its Enter in one go (from the phone, a paste, a script) no longer holds every later message as if the person were still writing it: messages from the phone, `hive ctl send` and other agents go in again.
- A message from the phone or `hive ctl send` to an agent that reports no turns (Codex, OpenCode, a custom agent) is typed at once again. Since a turn seen only on its screen held messages, one whose screen never showed its prompt again kept them forever.
- The Android app opens a computer's `hivemind://` link: tapped or opened from another app, it goes straight to pairing with it.
- In the Windows view and other views, a thin white line no longer shows around the panes, at their rounded corners and along the window's edges. The view's frame shows the wallpaper behind it again.
- Over a photo wallpaper, small bright blocks of the photo no longer appear around the canvas while agents work. The dot that travels along a pipe between two agents made the canvas redraw its wires 60 times a second, and on some Linux graphics drivers those redraws left pieces of the undimmed photo behind. The dot now moves without redrawing anything.
- Restarting the app keeps who started each worker: the Layers panel still nests it under the agent that spawned it, and the canvas still wires them. Its reports and approvals still go to that agent, its spawn depth still counts, and a supervised worker resumed later keeps its supervision. Before, all of it was lost at a restart, so workers showed flat and their replies reached no one.
- A spawned worker's first task always starts. The app starts every worker an agent or a workflow spawns, with its task on the agent's command line where it takes one there; the window only shows it. Before, the window started it, and when the tile reached the window before its task did, the agent started with no task (a mapreduce's reducer sat at an empty prompt). An agent that takes its task typed is not typed into while it is still loading. If it drops the Enter, Enter is pressed again only while the task still shows in its prompt, never on a chooser.
- A worker interrupted with Esc takes messages again: one sent after the interrupt was held until a later turn ended on its own.
- An agent whose turn was already running when the app started shows as working, not idle, and a message for it waits for the turn to end instead of being typed into it.
- `hive ctl read` on a worker whose agent has exited says `closed` at once instead of waiting out its timeout as "still working".
- A window or browser tab open while the app is rebuilt or upgraded reloads into the new build when it opens a dialog, instead of going blank.
- A widget that walks or animates, such as the dog, no longer drops the canvas's frame rate: the GPU now moves it and steps its sprite, so the page is no longer laid out and repainted on every frame.
- Settings → In a browser shows a link to copy for each of Other names, not only the 127.0.0.1 one, and a name may carry the port `tailscale serve` gives it (`box.tail1234.ts.net:8444`).
- Settings → Servers says what it is for, "Reach your devices from other networks", instead of "Relay and discovery; most people never change this".
- Over a wallpaper, a frame no longer gets a dark bar behind its name: only tiles' title bars are frosted.
- Layers: with several projects, each is set apart by a rule under a plain heading, as machines are, instead of a heading that looked like one more folder row.
- The website's `install.sh` and `install.ps1` are the current installers again, and CI fails when they or the site's design tokens fall behind.
- Settings → Shortcuts lists only keys that work: Diff review (⌘⇧G), New issue (⌘N) and toolbar keys 4 and 5 are gone, ⌘E is under On the canvas, and ⌘⇧F (fit a terminal to the screen) is listed.
- `hive ctl plugin open tool:…` prints the tile it opened instead of "switched to view undefined"; `hive ctl spawn --help` says it prints `{ tile }`.
- Terminals in other tabs no longer show scrambled letters after you switch tabs or come back to the window.
- Settings: a button is as wide as its label on every page, never stretched across it; a field's focus ring is the selection colour, not the theme's accent (Amber read as "waits on you").
- What a widget keeps (a counter's count) is no longer put back by another window or tab that edited the board before it saw the change.
- An agent busy with its first turn when the terminal service restarts is restored with the rest instead of ending: its screen is saved within 2 seconds even while it keeps printing.
