The status history keeps each exit's code and signal. Started from the phone, the new agent's screen says "Starting…" until it appears, not the name of the agent shown before.
- An agent no longer stays "waiting on you" after it went back to its prompt: a wait ends once its screen moves from the chooser to the prompt, however the chooser was answered.
- A permission on the phone and in Needs names the tool and the exact command or file it asks for, as Claude asks it, not only "permission".
- An agent restored when the app starts runs with the hooks and paths of the app installed now, not the ones it was first started with: after an update, a restored Claude kept the old permission hook (no answers from the phone) and a path into the old install. It keeps its session and what you chose for it (model, mode, effort).
- One screen read as the agent's prompt no longer ends what it waits on (a chooser redrawn, a repaint cutting its footer): the prompt has to stay for 2 seconds, as it already had to after work, for every agent.
- A terminal shown again (opened on the phone, or a tile scrolled back into view) asks its program to redraw without shrinking it by a row first, so a screen read at that moment is never missing its last line.
- A question Claude asks with AskUserQuestion shows on the phone with each option and what it means under it, plus "Type something" for any other answer; never Allow and Deny. A question that allows several answers is no longer offered as one choice. Opening the agent's terminal from the phone no longer turns its chooser into "idle".
- On the phone, an agent's Terminal no longer shows the waiting card under it, halving it: the terminal shows the chooser itself, and the header still says it waits. Back in Chat the card is there as before.
- On the phone, an agent whose manifest says no transcript the phone can read (one it maps but never says where to find included) opens in its terminal instead of a chat that stays empty.
- The phone page updates itself: it opens at once from the newest build it has, fetches a new one alongside, and the next open shows it, never files of two builds together. It never waits on the network to open.
- **Breaking:** `agent.answer` answers a question with `{answers: [{chosen: [n…]} | {text}]}`, one per question, instead of `{choice}`. On the phone, a wait with several questions can be answered: each shows its options (several can be picked where the question allows) and a line of its own, and one Send answers them all.
- A terminal opened on the phone is fitted to it from the start, every row of it in sight (its top line was cut off), and gets its own size back when you tap Fit or leave. While fitted, typing on the phone after typing on the desktop fits it again. With two phones fitted, the one that leaves gives the terminal back to the other, and a phone that leaves after the desktop typed gives it back to the desktop's size.
- On the phone, tapping the message box no longer pushes it up: it sits right on the keyboard on iOS Safari and Android Chrome, and the newest message stays in sight.
- On the phone, a page opened from a pairing link no longer ignores a quick tap on Pair: until the link is read, Pair says "Starting…". The field shows the pairing link itself, not the page's address.
- On the phone's Home, rows have no ⋯: hold a row (or right-click it) for what can be done to it, or swipe it left for Stop and Close. Start is a + and the search a plain field.
- On the phone's Home, a project's name stays at the top while you scroll its agents, and a project with one frame of the same name no longer lists that name twice.
- On the phone's Home, each row reads in plain text, monospace only for code (a command, or what is in backticks); the time is in even-width digits, and a hairline from where the text starts separates rows.
- On the phone, a permission names its tool and shows its command in monospace, four lines until you tap it; Allow and Deny are full-width rows, Allow first.
- On the phone, a chat breathes: 20 px between turns, 8 px between what the agent does within one, and what it says at 16/24.
- On the phone, scrolled up in a chat, a round button above the message box counts the new messages and takes you back to the newest.
- On the phone, a line above the message box says an agent is working and for how long ("working · 12s"), with a breathing dot.
- On the phone, tools an agent used one after another fold into one line, "Read 3 · Edit 2 · Run 1", that opens to the list. Tool lines are quiet sans text without ticks, red only when one failed, and only a command is in monospace.
- On the phone, an agent's header is its name and state in plain words, Terminal as an icon and Close in a ⋯ menu. Stop is the message box's button while the agent works and nothing is typed; once you type, it sends.
- On the phone, one place to type: while a question waits, the message box offers "Or type your own answer" and sends it as the answer; the question card keeps only its options.
- The phone page no longer flashes every screen on top of each other as it opens, and Start is ready at once from what your computer said last instead of "Still starting" until it is reached: about 300 ms on a phone-speed CPU instead of 900. Pressed before the computer is reached, Start waits for it.
- Starting an agent on the phone opens on its prompt, with one line saying which agent starts in which folder on which machine (the project you opened it from, else what you last started) that opens the choices, and Start always in sight. Two workspaces or folders of one name say where each is. Mode and model offer what the agent's command lists, as the desktop's settings do, with "As set in Settings" first.
- Home shows what each agent last said, not its status bar ("auto mode on", "plan mode on"), also after it was restored many times.
- Scrolling a terminal on the phone page works in full-screen programs: in Claude Code, less or vim a swipe goes to the program as the desktop's wheel does, so you scroll Claude's own history. Elsewhere a swipe shows earlier lines and stays there while output comes; "New output ↓", a key or a tap brings back the newest. A terminal opens with its last 200 lines and fetches more, up to 2,000, as you scroll to them, instead of 5,000 lines every time it is shown.
- A terminal takes the size of whoever typed in it last, at their first key: typing on the phone fits it to the phone, and the desktop's next key gives it back. Allow, Deny and choosing an option do not count as typing; Fit to phone still fits it at once.
- A worker spawned with `hive ctl spawn` that stops to wait for input (a question picker, a permission prompt) now tells the agent that spawned it, with the commands to see and answer it. Before, an unsupervised worker sat at its picker and its leader never heard about it.
- Changed: an agent's hooks (Claude Code, Codex, Droid, Kiro, Hermes) run through `hive-net hook`, a small native program, instead of the app's runtime: each one takes about 7 MB and 4 ms instead of about 64 MB and 70 ms (more on a busy machine), and that is all a permission waiting for your phone holds. Windows keeps the script.
- Changed: Allow, Deny and a question's option from your phone reach Claude Code through its own permission hook instead of typed keys, so an answer never lands before its chooser is drawn, and Deny can say why. Answering at the desktop still works as before; the first answer wins. An agent declares this in its manifest (`answer:` on its `input.requested` hook); others are answered with keys as before.
- Added: on the phone page, tap a tool an agent used to see its whole input (the full command, the whole edit) and what it gave back, up to 256 KB each instead of the first 2,000 characters.
- Added: start an agent from your phone in any folder of your projects, on this computer or a saved machine, from New agent or a project's heading: pick the machine, the folder (or one inside it) and an agent found there, and attach photos, which it gets as files next to your prompt. A folder without a frame gets one opened for it under its project. Stop or close an agent from its row's menu.
- Changed: the phone page opens faster, about 280 ms instead of 450 on a phone-speed CPU, as one script instead of fourteen with its fonts in hand before it is first drawn; and opening a long conversation no longer freezes the screen: the newest messages show at once and what was said before comes in above them.
- Changed: what an agent says reads the same on the phone page, Android and iOS, and shows more of it: italic, strikethrough, tables, links (https only, opened when tapped, bare addresses too), nested and task lists, quotes, rules, heading levels and a code block's language. An image shows as its words and is fetched only if you tap it; raw HTML shows as the text it is. Arabic and Hebrew paragraphs run from the right.
- Fixed: on the phone page and in the Android app, the message box no longer grows to two lines when the agent's name is long.
- Added: Views on the phone page: the views installed on your computer that work on a phone, each shown in a frame on an origin of its own, so it reaches nothing of the page (its key, storage, service worker or server) nor what a view before it left; a view that shows the board's widgets draws them there and presses them. The server shows them on a second port: `hive-net serve --phone <folder> --views <port>` (the compose files use 3341, and 8443 with a public name).
- Added: the phone page shows an agent's terminal one tap from its chat, and opens a terminal tile from Home: drawn at your computer's size and fitted to the phone's width (pinch to zoom, drag to pan), with Esc, Tab, arrows and Ctrl-C under it, and what you type going to it. Fit to phone sizes the terminal to the phone until you leave it, and it then takes back its size. A slow phone is sent the newest screen in place of output it fell behind on, never a growing queue.
- Fixed: Allow tapped on your phone the moment Claude Code asked could be lost: the keys went in before its chooser was drawn, and the phone said it was answered while Claude still asked. The computer now types the answer once the chooser is on screen and says it was answered only once the chooser takes it; otherwise the phone says "Not confirmed: check the chat." and never types it again.
- Added: hivemind on a phone without installing anything. Serve the page from your own network's server (`hive-net serve --phone <folder>`, the folder `crates/hive-web/build.sh` builds), and Settings ▸ Your devices ▸ Use on your phone, with Open in the phone's browser turned on, shows a QR code your phone's camera opens: the page asks, pairs as your phone on a tap, and shows what needs you (Allow, Deny, Approve a plan, or a reply), every agent by computer and workspace, and one agent's chat, where you send it a message or stop it. It looks like the Android app, can be added to the home screen, and opens offline with what it last knew. hivemind's own phone app reads the same QR code.
- Added: each release carries the phone page (`hive-phone-page.tar.gz`, with its hash in `hive-phone-page.sha256`), the self-hosting stack (`infra/compose.yml`) serves it at `/phone/`, and Use on your phone, with Open in the phone's browser on, shows the hash of the page your network's server serves and whether it is the page your hivemind release made.
- Added: the phone page starts an agent (workspace, folder, agent, model and mode, a prompt) and closes one, lists your other computers and hosts, scans the pairing QR code with the camera, and tells your computer when you forget it there. A page your computer no longer lets in offers to pair again.
- Changed: the QR code that opens the phone page is smaller and easier for a camera to read (version 26 to 21 on a machine with many network addresses): the link in it no longer lists your computer's addresses, which a browser cannot use. hivemind's own phone app still gets them.
- Changed: the phone page keeps up with hundreds of agents. Answering one redraws that card and that agent's row and nothing else (with 200 agents, the update took 56 ms on a phone-speed CPU and now takes about 10), rows off the screen cost nothing until scrolled to, and the working dots stop breathing once more than 40 agents work.
- Fixed: denying what an agent asks from your phone left it listed as needing you on the phone and the desktop until its next turn ended; an answer from another device now counts as you typing it, so the agent stops waiting at once. An answer is no longer typed when the agent's screen shows its prompt or something else it asks, nor twice when your phone cannot hear back (it says "Not confirmed: check the chat."), and the audit log records whether you allowed or denied.
- Added: on the phone page, a question an agent asks (Claude Code's AskUserQuestion) shows its question and one button per option, which answers it; it never shows Allow and Deny. A permission shows the tool and the exact command, file or address it is about, whole, above Allow and Deny. An agent's manifest says the keys that choose an option (`choose`) and how its session file names the questions (`asks`).
- Changed: the phone page opens on Home, which lists what the desktop's Layers panel does, in its order: each machine, project, frame and tile, agents first, what an agent started under it, your pins on top. What needs you comes first, with its answers, then what is at work; each row says what the agent last said, or what a terminal last printed. Search by name or agent, and pick a machine when there is more than one. The Needs you and Agents tabs are gone. A canvas of 200 tiles opens in about 0.4 s on a phone-speed CPU, and one agent's change redraws its row alone.
- Fixed: after answering an agent that reports no turns with a single key, a message you sent it next was held as if you were still typing; an answer's keys are no longer taken for your draft.
- Fixed: a phone that opened the chat of an agent that had not said anything yet never heard what it said next; it now follows the agent's conversation from its first line.
- Fixed: an agent installed by an older Hivemind or by `hive agents install` never updated from the catalog, because nothing recorded that the catalog put it there, so a fixed manifest never reached it: Codex kept stopping on its hook review. An installed manifest whose bytes are a file of the catalog's repository is the catalog's own and follows it; one you edited still stays as you left it.
- Changed: opening Settings the first time no longer waits on its code (about 0.6 s on a busy machine, now about 0.1 s): it is fetched while the app is idle. A wait shorter than 0.3 s shows nothing; a longer one shows a spinner and what it is waiting for, the same everywhere.
- Fixed: Settings pages that said a thing twice or drew it twice: Programs' title and first heading were the same words, About showed "Checking…" as its status and as its button, and Shortcuts ended a group with two rules. Share's "anyone with the link" is a switch like every other setting, not a bare checkbox.
- Changed: in Settings ▸ Plugins, the agent that is not the default offers "Make default" (a view, "Use") when you point at its row, instead of an empty circle in a box.
- Fixed: Settings opened at a section (Plugins from the rail) put the keyboard on Profile; it starts on the section it opened at.
- Changed: a frame on the canvas is a hairline and a faint fill with its name at the top left; the dashed border and the bar across its top are gone, and its ⋯ menu shows when you point at it.
- Fixed: Fit to view (Esc), a focus or a zoom asked for while the camera was still settling from the last move could be cut short, leaving the canvas stopped part way.
- Fixed: a tab restored from the rail in the Windows view could come back minimized when you switched views at once on a busy machine: the view's last saved layout was dropped as it was let go.
- Fixed: in the Windows view, clicking the tab already shown left the keyboard in the tab strip; it goes back to the terminal.
- Fixed: `hive host` read its settings from its own data folder, not the settings.json the app and `hive config` keep, so on a Mac it ignored them; and it read them again only when an agent was added or removed, not when they changed.
- Added: a tool plugin can bring a service, a program the app keeps running while the plugin is on (`"service": {"command", "role"}` in `hivemind-tool.json`), such as an orchestrator or a dashboard. It drives the workspaces picked for it on the plugin's page through the drive socket, with a token made for each run and taken back when it stops, and none of the app's own. A failing exit starts it again after 1 s, doubling to a minute. Its state and what it prints are on its page. `hive host` runs them too, with the workspaces set by `hive plugins workspaces <tool:id> <folder>…`. `hive ctl` uses `HIVE_DRIVE_TOKEN` when it is set, and a program whose grant reaches one workspace need not name it.
- Fixed: `agent.list` straight after a change (a rename) could list the agent as it was; what the feed has waiting is worked out before it is read.
- **Breaking:** the control plane's socket (`hcp.sock`) carries only what an agent's own plugin and hooks say: `agent.reply`, `agent.report`, `agent.await_approval`, `review.open` and the `agent.event` notification. The rest is the workspace API on the drive socket, as `hive ctl` uses it; `tile.spawn_agent`, `tile.list`, `tile.list_frames`, `tile.close`, `agent.send`, `agent.send_keys`, `agent.read`, `agent.approve`, `agent.list` and `host.people` answer `UNKNOWN_METHOD`. `agent.send` takes a prompt of up to 32768 characters, over several lines. `hive share`, `hive people` and `hive join` use the drive socket of the app or `hive host`. The pi plugin's tools run `hive ctl`; `hive_list_tiles` is `hive_list_agents`, and `hive_send` has no `submit`.
- Fixed: a worker an agent starts with `hive ctl spawn` runs in its agent's unattended mode again; one you or a program start keeps its prompts.
- Fixed: `hive ctl read` after `hive ctl send` waits for the turn the message starts, not one that ended before it.
- **Breaking:** `hive ctl` drives the app on its drive socket, the workspace API, as the agent whose terminal it runs in (in that agent's workspace) or as you (in the open workspace your folder is in, the only one open, or the one `--repo` names). `spawn` and `open-tool` print `{"tile": …}`, not `{"tileId": …}`. `hive ctl list` is gone: `hive ctl agents` lists the workspace's agents, and `frames` lists where one may be started (`id`, `name`, `machine`). `sessions` lists one folder's, so `--all` is gone. `send` and `close` to a tile no agent runs exit 5. Agents are given the socket as `HIVE_DRIVE_SOCK`. `approve` answers through `agent.answer`, whose `always` (workspace API 0.23) decides a supervised tool for that worker from then on. `stream` follows what the agent prints from the moment it starts, and `--lines N` prints the last N lines it printed first; `--since` and byte offsets are gone, and `--json` prints `{seq, chunk, printed?}`. A program reads those last lines with `agent.output`, and may show a session with `terminal.open {attachOnly}` without saying where or what it runs. The control plane no longer streams output or statuses (`agent.stream/subscribe`, `status/subscribe`). `workflow` runs in the CLI from `agent.start`, `agent.read` and `agent.close`, so a person runs one too (`--repo`); each result names its worker `tile`, not `tileId`, and a worker that closed before replying is `status: "closed"`. The control plane has no `workflow.run`. `spawn --frame` and `workflow --frame` take a frame's id or its name as `frames` lists it, in any case. `plugin open` of a tool prints `{"tile": …}`. `report` and `open-review` still go to the control plane, for now.
- Fixed: on a Mac, `hive ctl`, `hive attach` and `hive grant` outside an agent's terminal looked for the app's token and sockets in `~/.config/hivemind`, not in `~/Library/Application Support/hivemind` where the app keeps them.
- Fixed: `hive ctl disconnect <src>` with no `<dst>` printed its usage instead of removing every pipe out of `<src>`.
- Internal: the CLI's tests no longer inherit the agent tile they may be run from, so none reaches the app the suite was started in.
- Fixed: the host could act on a guest's or a phone's messages out of order: one naming a folder was checked on disk first, so a message sent after it, such as the next board edit or call, could run before it. They are now taken in the order sent.
- Added: the workspace API, and so a program or an agent on `drive.sock`, does what only `hive ctl` did: name an agent (`agent.rename`), type keys to it (`agent.keys`), wait for its next reply (`agent.read`), list the sessions it can resume (`agent.sessions`), open a tool's tile (`tool.open`) and pipe one agent's replies to another (`link.connect`, `link.disconnect`), each checked against the caller's role and workspace. The machine's own methods (rescans, settings, plugins, joining) are there too, for the person and the agents on this machine (joining the person's alone), never for a guest's device or a program (workspace API 0.23).
- Internal: `apps/desktop/scripts/e2e.sh` runs the e2e suite, or named specs, headless with its log and summary, one run at a time; `--fresh N` runs a spec N times with a new app each time, to measure a flaky one.
- **Breaking:** one set of error codes for the workspace API, the drive socket and `hive ctl` (workspace API 0.23). The API adds `NOT_FOUND`, `UNSUPPORTED`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `DEPTH_EXCEEDED`, `TIMEOUT` and `UNAVAILABLE`, so a program on `drive.sock` can tell a missing tile or a full spawn queue from a failure; a frame, workspace, agent or view that is not there is now `NOT_FOUND` rather than `BAD_REQUEST`. The control plane's own codes go: `TILE_NOT_FOUND` is `NOT_FOUND`, `UNAUTHORIZED` is `FORBIDDEN`, `APP_NO_RENDERER` is `UNAVAILABLE` and `INTERNAL` is `FAILED`, in `hive ctl --json` too; exit statuses are unchanged.
- Internal: e2e runs under xvfb opened the app's windows on a Wayland desktop anyway, over whatever you were doing and taking the keyboard, and could show its notifications there. The e2e config now keeps Electron on the X display xvfb-run gives and off your session bus.
- Internal: the widgets e2e spec pressed a widget's button in a view the moment it was drawn, before the window routed the pointer into the view's frame, so about one run in seven the click landed on the window instead. It now points at the button until the frame says the pointer reached it.
- Added: an agent can drive its own workspace on `drive.sock` with its tile's token (`HCP_TOKEN`), at the role `agents`, while its tile is there (workspace API 0.23). What it starts there is its own, as with `hive ctl spawn`: wired to it on the board, reporting to it, at most three levels deep, and a tool use it supervises is its to answer.
- Internal: the drive-programs e2e spec still expected workspace API 0.21.
- Internal: an e2e spec drives the drive API's "done when" end to end: a program with a scoped grant opens a worktree frame, starts Claude there, answers its trust screen, reads its reply, session and conversation, supervises it and is refused `git.push`. The stand-in Claude now keeps its session id and transcripts where Claude does.
- Fixed: `hive host`, or the app, started from inside an agent's terminal gave every shell it then ran that agent's identity and token, so `hive ctl` in those shells acted as the agent. A terminal's identity is now only its own.
- Internal: the `hive network` test still chose the hosted network, which is gone.
- Changed: a link in a terminal (a file path, an address, a link a program prints) opens on Ctrl+click, ⌘+click on a Mac, as in VS Code. A plain click is a click in the terminal, so clicking into one to type no longer opens the file under the pointer. Pointing at a link says so.
- Fixed: an address clicked in a terminal opened nowhere while the Browser tool was switched off. It opens in your system's browser.
- Fixed: a terminal tab switched away from just after its pane changed size was told the size of its hidden box, so its program redrew for a size nobody saw, and again when shown.
- Added: `agent.list` says the session each agent is in (`session`, workspace API 0.22), as its agent last said, kept after it exits. A new one, after `/clear` or resuming another, comes as `agent.changed`; `agent.start`'s `resume` goes on with it.
- Internal: the Queue view spec took the view's own cursor as proof the app had selected that tile, and left the view before the app heard; it now waits until the app shows the tile docked, which the view asks for after selecting it.
- New: a screen an agent asks something on can say what it is. An agent's manifest may name the screen a waiting rule reads (`says: trust`), and give keys under that name (`answer.trust`). The agents list (`waiting.says`), the needs list and phone notices say which screen it is, and Allow / Deny type that screen's keys, never a tool prompt's. Claude Code's "do you trust this folder" and unreadable-settings screens are named this way, read from claude 2.1.296; they reach installed copies with the next Claude plugin update.
- Fixed: clicking Add (or reopening any dialog) just after picking from it did nothing, or opened a dialog that took no clicks until Escape. A closing dialog now lets the page under it take the click, and comes back whole when opened again mid-close.
- Fixed: a selected terminal whose renderer was rebuilt (the GPU dropping it, another terminal taking its place) took the keyboard back from a field you had moved to, such as the Layers search.
- Fixed: a phone asking for a view made only for a desk is told the view is not for a phone, rather than that it does not exist.
- Internal: the host-view, phone-pairing and guest-machine e2e specs had fallen behind the app (Settings opened from an event, the features a phone is offered, a prompt that ended in `#` only as root).
- Fixed: switching between tabs in the Windows view, or showing a hidden terminal again, made its program redraw as if resized, even when nothing had happened in it meanwhile. A terminal is now sent its screen again, and its program asked to redraw, only when it missed output while hidden; and a tab put away keeps its size until it is shown somewhere else, where it was resized to its canvas tile and back on every switch.
- Internal: five e2e specs had fallen behind the app (the frame menu's Arrange, Terminal in Add, the Layers row's status dot, the Windows view in its own frame) and failed every run.
- **Breaking:** the workspace API's `plan.list`, `plan.decide`, `plan.review` and `plan.decided` are gone (0.21). A plan an agent waits on is in `agent.list` (`waiting.plan`) and is answered with `agent.answer`, like a supervised tool use; an answer that comes second hears who answered first (`by`), and the `answered` event says the decision and who gave it. `hive ctl open-review` runs in the agent's tile, or names it with `--tile`.
- Fixed: an agent that went from one kind of wait straight to another (a permission, then a question) kept the first wait's name, so an answer to the second could land on the first or be refused as late.
- Internal: `pnpm test` stopped at the tool-plugin SDK, which has no tests, and skipped every package after it; and the phone core's tests still expected the hosted network, which is gone.
- Internal: the app's window and the dev bridge's browser page get `window.hive`'s workspace calls from one module, and its type is declared once. The bridge's hand-kept copy had drifted (no presence, widgets, agents or views) and its catch-all answered `null` where a list was due, which crashed the canvas on load; a call it does not answer now fails instead.
- Fixed: a program on the drive socket that started an agent just after the app opened a folder was refused with "no workspace is open" until the window had laid the folder out. A workspace the app holds is open from the start.
- Fixed: new terminals started with the environment the background daemon had when it first started, so a tool installed or a PATH changed since (and found by the agent check's Check again) was missing in them until the daemon restarted. Each new terminal now starts from the app's environment as it is now (and `hive run`'s from its shell's).
- Changed: every terminal runs in the background daemon, the dev bridge's too. `HIVEMIND_PTY_DAEMON=0`, which ran terminals inside the app with none of the agents' hooks, is gone, and the app and `hive daemon` start the daemon one way.
- Changed: no second Settings button in the window's corner where the Layers panel already has one (the Windows view, the canvas with its toolbar off). Views without Layers and without a toolbar keep it there.
- Fixed: the terminal daemon kept the app's own pipes and sockets open for as long as it ran, after the app had quit. It now lets go of them as it starts, whether the app or `hive daemon` started it (Linux).
- Added: a program on `drive.sock` can supervise the agents it starts. `agent.start` takes `supervise` (`true`, `"all"` or a list of tools), `name`, `resume` and `report`. A supervised agent's tool use then waits in `agent.list` with the tool, what it would do (`rm -rf dist`) and when it gives up, and the program allows or denies it with `agent.answer`; if nobody answers, the agent asks at its own prompt as before. What an agent it started reports with `hive ctl report` comes to it as an `agent.event` of kind `reported`, and each agent says who started it (`parent`). Workspace API 0.20, spec/agents.md 0.5, spec/needs.md 0.6.
- Added: a program can put agents in their own folder or worktree. `frame.open` (workspace API 0.20) opens a frame on a folder of the workspace, or on a new worktree for a branch, starting from `base` if given, and gives back the same frame when asked again. `frame.close` closes it, ending its tiles and removing its worktree only when asked; git still refuses to remove a worktree with uncommitted changes. The window places such a frame in the next free spot. Guests and programs can now reach a workspace's worktrees, which live beside the repo folder, not inside it.
- Added: programs on this machine can drive your workspaces without the app's own token. `hive grant create <name> -w <folder> --role agents` prints a token for one program; it connects to `drive.sock` and gets the workspace API for those workspaces at that role, so an orchestrator can start, follow and answer agents but cannot push, share the workspace or write files. `hive grant list` shows each grant and when it was last used, `hive grant revoke <id>` takes one back and closes its connections, and `--expires 30d` lets one run out (workspace API 0.20).
- Fixed: Codex opened every tile on "Hooks need review" for the hooks Hivemind adds. An agent's manifest can now say `hooks.trust: codex`: the app then hands Codex, at launch, its own record of trust for exactly those hooks, so it never asks about them, and still asks about any other hook, yours or a repository's. Codex is no longer started with `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`, which skipped the review of every hook. Needs the Codex agent from HiveHub that declares it.
- Added: in the Windows view, drag a tab onto another pane's tabs to move it there, or onto the edge of a pane to split it; Escape cancels. Along its own tabs a drag reorders them.
- Fixed: the Windows view's keys (Ctrl+\\ to split, Ctrl+Shift+Enter to maximize, Ctrl+Alt+arrows to move between panes) did nothing while a terminal had focus, and Ctrl+\\ reached the shell. They now work wherever focus is.
- Added: a view's command can name the keys that run it (`"keys": "mod+shift+k"` in its manifest). While the view is shown they work anywhere in the app, terminals included, and in the view's own frame (view protocol 1.13, `hello.keys`). Keys a terminal or the app answers (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Shift+V, a letter without Alt, arrows, Escape, zoom) are refused.
- Added: the Windows view splits like an editor. Split a pane right or down from its tab bar (or Ctrl+\\ and Ctrl+Shift+\\), drag the divider between panes, double-click it to make them equal, maximize a pane, close one into its neighbour, and move between panes with Ctrl+Alt+arrows. Each pane has its own tabs, and the arrangement is kept per person. An agent can do the same with `hive ctl plugin call windows split-right` and the view's other commands.
- Added: view protocol 1.13: a surface rect can let the pointer through to the view while it drags (`inert`), and `setLayout(data, { now: true })` saves at once instead of after 250 ms.
- Fixed: a view's rounded surfaces (`radius`) were dropped by the view SDK before they reached the app.
- Changed: the Windows view's tabs and the tile they show are two rounded cards, level with the Layers panel, instead of a flat strip across the top.
- Added: a view can round the corners of a tile it shows (`radius` on a surface rect, view protocol 1.13).
- Fixed: "Add a tool or widget" in the Layers panel or a frame's menu did nothing in a view without the toolbar (Windows). Add is now one dialog wherever it is opened, toolbar or not; what it adds on the canvas goes where the pointer was when it opened.
- Added: the Layers panel closes from a button in its header, and reopens from the same corner; the shortcut is Ctrl+B (⌘B on macOS).
- Changed: "Join a shared workspace…" is in the Layers panel's title menu, next to Open a folder and Recent folders, and opens straight on the join form.
- Added: a view's manifest says where the app's toolbar goes, `"toolbar": "top" | "bottom" | "hidden" | "off"`; Settings → Views still overrides it per view.
- Changed: the Windows view has no toolbar; its terminal runs to the bottom of the window. The Layers panel's title menu switches views ("Show as") in every view.
- Changed: the toolbar's frame button has a dashed-square icon.
- Changed: icons are Hugeicons, rounded, at one stroke weight everywhere. Plugins draw the same icons by importing them from `@hivemind/app-ui`.
- Changed: "Add a tool or widget" has an icon of its own instead of the plugin puzzle piece.
- Fixed: the toolbar's icon buttons showed their shortcut as a tiny number under the icon, which read as a count. The shortcut is in the button's tooltip.
- Changed: badges (the UI kit's, which plugins use too) and the smallest buttons use the app's type scale, 10px and 11px instead of 10.5px. The Diff tile's file counts are plain numbers instead of 8.5px white-on-accent pills.
- Changed: Settings opens as a sheet over the workspace, with round corners, the workspace dimmed behind it, instead of covering the whole window.
- Fixed: in the Layers panel, every agent that had finished a turn showed its name in bold, so the panel read as all headings. Unread is now a small blue dot after the name.
- Fixed: the Layers panel listed the workspace's own frame under "Groups" and left "Projects" empty. A project is a folder: frames are grouped by the folder they run in (their own, or the workspace's), and with one folder there is no heading to say which.
- Changed: the Layers panel and the Appearance panel are sheets set in from the window's edges, with round corners.
- Fixed: an agent's screen could be garbled after switching project or view, its text running into older text ("Theeotel-collectorNdepends…"). The screen it was shown again was a copy, and an agent that repaints only what changed (Claude Code) patched its own idea of the screen over that copy. Whenever a session's screen is shown from a copy (attaching to a running session, joining it, showing it again), the host now makes the program repaint all of it, once.
- Fixed: resizing a terminal now resizes its grid and tells the program in the same step: the first size of a resize burst at once, then only the one it ends at. Before, the grid could be one width while the program drew at another, which wraps its lines and leaves what it meant to erase. The session's size coming back no longer re-measures the tile mid-drag (it made a drag resize the program on every frame).
- Changed: a frame's menu offers **Add a tool or widget…**, the Add menu adding into that frame, instead of its own **Open ▸** list.
- Fixed: in single-choice menus (the Layers View menu's Status, Group by and Sort by, Recents' grouping) the chosen option was marked by a dot too small to see; it shows a check, as in multi-choice menus.
- New: the Layers panel's **Recents** groups past sessions by agent (the default), by project or by date, or not at all, from **By agent ⌄** at its top (`hive config set views.layers.recentsBy project`). Each group folds and shows its newest five with its own **Show more**.
- Changed: the Layers panel has a rail on its left edge: **Tiles**, **Recents** and **Machines** each get the panel to themselves, with **Plugins** and **Settings** at its foot. In Tiles, frames with a folder are listed under **Projects** (their worktrees under them) and frames without one under **Groups**; grouping by project is the default. The other workspaces on this computer moved from a Projects section into the title's menu. **New machine** and the machines moved to the Machines pane; the View menu's *Show idle machines* is gone (setting `views.layers.showMachines` removed).
- Fixed: a frame's ⋯ menu on the canvas opened away from its button, further the more the canvas was zoomed or panned. It opens at the button now.
- Changed: the Layers panel, its menus and a frame's header set text in one scale (15 the title, 14 a row, 12 a heading or menu, 11 a count or key, 10 a status chip), down from ten sizes; a menu no longer repeats the row's name as an uppercase label. A unit test fails when a size off the scale is added there.
- New: in the Layers panel a tile that finished something while you looked elsewhere is unread (its name in bold) until you select it; **Mark as unread** / **Mark as read** (U) in its menu, and **Unread** in the View menu's Status. **Snooze** (30 minutes, 1 hour, 2 hours, tomorrow at 9:00) moves a tile to **Snoozed** until then; it comes back early when it needs you or you select it, or with **Wake now**. Both are yours, per workspace.
- Changed: Ctrl/⌘+1…9 go to the tiles in the order the Layers panel lists them (pinned first, then as grouped and nested), not the order they were opened; held a moment, Ctrl/⌘ shows each of the first nine rows its number.
- Fixed: resizing a terminal (dragging a tile, a panel or the window, switching views) left an agent's old borders on screen. The program in it was told every size the drag passed through, nine or more, and redrew at each; it now hears one resize, at the size the drag ends at, while the screen still follows every frame. A resized terminal is redrawn whole, its glyphs are rebuilt when the window regains focus, and a terminal you scrolled up in stays where you were instead of jumping to the bottom.
- Fixed: in the Windows view and every other view that docks tiles, the bottom tool island covered the last lines of the active tile (an agent's prompt and status line). The island now says how much of the window it covers, and those views keep their tiles above it.
- Changed: a frame has one menu, the same wherever it opens: the ⋮ on its row in the Layers panel (shown on hover; right-click still works) and the ⋯ on its header on the canvas. New agent ▸ (your agents, default first) and Open ▸ (every tool, as Add lists them), then where it runs (Open folder…, Run on a machine…, Close folder, Disconnect machine, Detach worktree…, Worktree ▸, Git ▸), then Arrange ▸ (canvas only), Rename (R), Color ▸ and Delete frame… (D). The canvas header keeps its name, folder, branch, machine, colour and the needs-you dot; its eight buttons and their popups are gone.
- Changed: Add ▸ Tools and a frame's Open ▸ list tools by category, then by name; the ones that ship with the app no longer come first.
- Fixed: in the Windows view, the active tab's body no longer repeats the tile's name in a bar of its own under the tab; a terminal takes that room. A view that draws its own chrome around a tile (`chrome: "none"`) gets every tile without the bar that only names it; a browser keeps its address bar. Minimize and close sit at the right end of a tab, not after its name.
- Changed: the Layers panel's **New agent** asks which agent, from the ones installed here, with the default first and its key (2); it starts where the 2 key would (the selected frame, else the one you pick). With none installed it opens Settings ▸ Agents.
- New: in the Layers panel, what an agent started (`hive ctl spawn`) is listed under it, as deep as it goes, and folds away with the chevron beside it. A started tile in another frame, or one the View menu leaves its starter out of, is listed on its own.
- Changed: the Layers panel lists Projects last, after the tiles, the canvas's loose tiles and Recents. Its title, headings and rows start on one edge; New agent leads the actions and the rest are quieter; a shortcut shows as a keycap on the row you point at; icons take a 1.5 stroke beside regular text. "No tiles open" no longer shows above frames that are listed.
- Fixed: the Layers panel's **Recents** was empty in a workspace whose frames have no folder of their own (a plain group): it now also reads the workspace's folder, where those agents run. It also read the installed agents before the app had loaded them, and now reads again once they have.
- Changed: the empty canvas leads with **New agent**, wearing the default agent's own logo, and offers **Open folder**; it no longer offers "Initialize workspace here…", and the app's own initialize dialog is gone. Issues are the Issues tool's: in a folder that keeps none yet, it offers **Set up issues** with the prefix their ids start with.
- New (view protocol 1.12): `hm.issues.status()` says whether the workspace keeps issues yet and suggests a prefix; `hm.issues.setUp(prefix)` starts keeping them in the workspace's folder (permission `workspace:issues`).
- New: deleting a frame that holds tiles asks what becomes of them: **Keep them** (they stay running, loose on the canvas) or **Close** them with the frame. The rail menu and the frame's × both ask.
- New: the Layers panel's **Canvas** section (tiles in no frame) has **Close all**, with a confirm.
- Fixed: the Layers panel's Canvas section listed each tile of a deleted frame once per tile in it (a frame of 9 tiles showed each 9 times). Deleting a frame now also clears its tiles' membership.
- Fixed: the Layers hover card's padding, and it opens beside the panel instead of over the row.
- New (view protocol 1.11): each tile in `structure` carries its `icon`, the mark the app draws for it, as one `<svg>` in `currentColor`. The Windows view's tabs use it: agents show their logo instead of "✦", and a tab shows its full name on hover only when the name is cut short.
- New: the Layers panel shows **Projects** (every workspace on this computer, with how many agents need you or work there; another one opens in this window) and **Recents** (this workspace's past agent sessions, newest first; a click resumes one in its group). It is there for an empty workspace too.
- Changed: the Layers panel reads like a sidebar. Its title is the workspace's name (⌄: recent projects, open a project, hide the panel); search and a bell (it counts the agents that need you and goes to the next) are its only icons; **New agent**, **New project**, **New machine**, **Add a tool or widget** and **Plugins** are named rows; the tiles' heading says how they are grouped (**By machine ⌄**) and opens the View menu. One type and icon scale throughout, and a tool with no icon shows a drawn window instead of a character.
- New: the Layers panel can pin tiles (Pinned, at the top) and has a **View** menu: filter by status, last activity and machine; group by machine, group, date, status or agent, or not at all; sort by canvas order, last activity or name; show or hide idle machines. Each row has a ⋮ menu (Pin P, Rename R, Close D) and a hover card. The View menu is kept in settings (`views.layers`); pins and folded sections per workspace, for you alone. What the panel lists is worked out by `@hivemind/core/layers`.
- Changed: in Settings ▸ People, one press makes the invite link and copies it (**Copy invite link**), instead of making it first and copying it second.
- New: **Make your own…** at the bottom of the Add menu and in Settings ▸ Plugins. Choose widget, tool or view, say what it should do, then copy the prompt for any agent or start an agent here with it; the agent creates the plugin with `hive <kind>s new`, follows its `PROMPT.md`, and checks it through its own commands.
- New: `hive widgets new` and `hive tools new` write a `PROMPT.md`, as `hive views new` did: what to build goes at the top, then how to install it, show it (`hive ctl plugin open`) and check it through its own commands, and the kind's whole guide. Hand it to a coding agent and it can build and test the plugin by itself.
- Fixed: codex asked "Hooks need review" again whenever another install or build of the app started it (the installed app, a dev build, a newer version), because each wrote its own paths into the hook commands. Skipped, the hooks did not run, so the app never learned the session and could not resume it, nor follow its turns. The commands now name no path (each app sets them in codex's environment), so they are trusted once. Trust them once more after updating.
- New: `hive ctl plugin open <record>` gets a plugin running so an agent can call its commands: it opens a tool's tile (`tool:owner/name`), adds a widget to the board (`widget:@owner/name`) or switches to a view (`view:id`).
- Fixed: in the Windows view, a strip with more tabs than fit could not reach the ones past the edge. Tabs now shrink as in a browser but never past a readable name, minimize and close show only on the tab under the pointer (the selected one keeps its name too), the mouse wheel scrolls the strip, and the selected tab is scrolled into view.
- Changed: Settings sits at the right end of the toolbar, as in a call's control bar, with a new icon; where a view's toolbar is off, collapsed or hidden by zen mode, it shows in the window's top-right corner. Share is a page in Settings (Settings → People) instead of a button and a dialog of its own.
- New: an agent can run what a plugin offers. A view, tool or widget declares `"commands"` in its manifest and answers them with `hm.onCommand` (view protocol 1.10); `hive ctl plugin commands` lists what the running plugins offer, and `hive ctl plugin call <plugin> <command> '<json>'` runs one and prints its answer. Examples: the Progress widget (`examples/widgets/progress`), the Agent board view (`examples/views/agent-board`), and `list` / `failures` in the Worktrees and CI runs tools.
- **Breaking:** the toolbar's Terminal and Code buttons are gone: both open from Add, as every tool does. The `1` and `3` keys still open them. A saved toolbar that lists `terminal` or `explorer` drops them.
- New: `useTileState(hm, key, initial)` in a tool plugin's `sdk.js`: `useState` kept with the tile across restarts. The example tools keep what was chosen, their filters, tabs and search with it.
- New: a tool plugin can ship an icon (`"icon": "icon.svg"` on each tool), drawn in the text colour wherever the app shows the tool. Issues and the example tools have one.
- Changed: the Add menu has a category panel beside its list, and keeps one size: the list scrolls.
- Changed: a plugin tile that is loading shows a spinner in the middle of it, saying what it waits for, instead of "Loading…" at the top. Plugins get it as `Loading` from `sdk.js` and the UI kit.
- New: a view, tool or widget says what it is for with `"category"` in its manifest: code, git, agents, productivity, monitoring, team, web, fun or other (the default). A value not on the list is refused.
- Changed: the Add menu offers every tool beside the widgets, grouped by category, with a search box and All, Tools and Widgets tabs, on the Layers panel's material instead of the see-through toolbar's. Popovers take the same material.
- Changed: text sent to an agent asks which one in an opaque card: agents grouped by frame, searchable when there are many, the new one below, chosen by click or by arrows and Enter. "Send to claude" is now "Send to agent", and the frame picker is the same card.
- Fixed: text on the board read blurred after scrolling it with a trackpad: the board now comes to rest on whole pixels.
- Changed: one Code tile. The separate Diff tile is gone: its review is the Code tile's **Changes**, and the toolbar's Explorer button is now **Code**. A Diff or Workbench tile on a board opens as the Code tile, and opening Code in a frame that has one focuses it instead of adding a second. A branch handed off to you opens in a Code tile on its changes.
- **Breaking:** the Diff toolbar action, the `4` key, Ctrl/⌘+Shift+G and a view's `spawnVis("diff")` are removed.
- New: two example tool plugins in `examples/tools`: CI runs (GitHub Actions runs, a failed job's lines, rerun, hand the failure to an agent) and Worktrees (every worktree's uncommitted changes, ahead and behind the base, its diffs; remove the merged ones).
- Changed: Issues is a tool plugin that comes with the app (`hivemind/issues`), built on the same SDK as anyone's: the Issues tile, with New issue and each issue's dialog in it, opened from a frame's Open menu. **Breaking:** the title bar's New issue button, ⌘N, and the toolbar's Issues button and its 5 key are gone; the issues themselves, and `hive new | list | show | update`, are unchanged.
- New: view protocol 1.9 — a view, a tool's tile or a widget reads the workspace's issues live (`hm.onIssues`) and calls its issue methods (`hm.issues.read | create | update | setState | comment | delete | link | unlink | move | workspaces`); all of it needs the permission `workspace:issues`, and every write is to the plugin's own workspace.
- New: a tool plugin draws with the app's own UI kit (buttons, inputs, dialogs, popovers, menus, Markdown, notifications), imported from `hm-plugin://host/ui.js`, so it looks like the app.
- Changed: the Windows view is a view package that comes with the app, built on the same protocol as anyone's, with the app's Layers rail beside it. Installing a view with the id `windows` replaces it. Which tabs you minimized starts afresh once.
- New: a view's manifest can ask for the app's Layers rail beside it (`"layers": true`), with every frame action, and keep what it saves on this device instead of in the shared workspace (`"layout": "personal"`).
- **Breaking:** a widget reads and acts on the workspace through `hive.hm`, the client a view and a tool's tile have, held to its widget.json's permissions: `hive.hm.on("structure")`, `subscribeStatus`, `onEvents`, `commands.spawnAgent`, `prompt`. `hive.workspace` and the `agents` and `agent` events are gone.
- Changed: a view's `hm.on("structure" | "names" | "selection" | "settings" | "theme" | …)` tells a listener added later how things are now, at once.
- New: widgets on your phone, and wherever else a widget is shown but not run: a press there reaches its code. A view can show the workspace's widgets (view protocol 1.8: `hm.onWidgets`), and press them only with the permission `workspace:widgets`, asked for at install (`hm.pressWidget`); install `@dip497/widgets` on the computer that hosts a workspace to see them on the phone (workspace API 0.19: `widget.press`).
- New: widgets run on a workspace with no window open on its host: a `hive host` hands them to the person's own laptop or desktop that has the workspace open, with the host's widgets (workspace API 0.19: `widget.packages`; `widget.run` from the person's other devices). Someone else's device never runs them.
- Fixed: a widget no longer starts again (losing a press, or what it was doing) each time another setting changes, such as switching views.
- New: a widget from HiveHub shows Update when a newer version is published; a published widget must say its `version`. `hive widgets new <name>` starts one, with `hive.d.ts` so an editor checks its code against the API it is given.
- Changed: Settings has one Plugins page for agents, views, widgets and tools, yours or on HiveHub, with one search and a kind filter. Each plugin has one page: its settings, its README, and what it can do. The Agents, Views, Tools, Browse and Installed pages are gone; links to them open the new ones.
- New: `hive plugins list | show | enable | disable | set | reset` switches any plugin on or off and sets what its settings take, checked against them (`hive plugins set view:@you/board columns all`).
- New: a view can declare settings in `hivemind-view.json` (`settings`: boolean, number, text or choice fields). The app draws them on its page and the view reads them as `hm.settings` (view protocol 1.7). Widgets' options use the same fields, choices included.
- New: widgets on HiveHub. Install one from Settings ▸ Plugins ▸ On HiveHub, after a review that says what it may do, or with `hive widgets install @owner/name`; `hive widgets list | install <folder> | remove` manage the rest. A published widget is `@owner/name` and says so in its widget.json. **Breaking:** HiveHub's index lists widgets, which an app before this one cannot read: update to see the catalog. A kind the app does not know is now skipped instead of failing the whole catalog.
- New: a widget shows its own icon in the Add menu and in Settings (`"icon": "icon.svg"` in its widget.json); the toolbar's Add button is a shapes icon, with Arrow set apart below the widgets.
- New: widgets can be switched off; one switched off is not offered and does not run. Widgets now live in `~/.config/hivemind/widgets`, beside views.
- **Breaking:** plugin on/off and options are stored as one record per plugin (`plugins.state` in settings.json), read from the old keys once (`settings.json.bak` keeps the file as it was). `hive config set tools.enabledPlugins …` no longer switches a tool; use `hive plugins enable tool:hivemind/web`.
- Changed: the toolbar's Board button is now Add, listing every widget alike (Sticky note, Checklist and Text first, then yours) and, on the canvas, Arrow. The 8 and 9 keys no longer add a note or checklist. Notes and checklists already on your boards become widgets as they are read; a new one lands a step off any already there instead of on top of it.
- New: `hive ctl agents` lists a workspace's agents and what each waits on; `--watch` follows them, printing each turn ended, wait and answer as it happens. It reads the app's agent feed, the one widgets read too (workspace API 0.18: `agent.list`, `agent.since`, `agent.changed`, `agent.event`).
- New: widgets, small live cards your own code draws, floating above every view where you drag them. Add a widget folder (`widget.json` + `widget.js`) in Settings ▸ Plugins, then the toolbar's Add; the gear sets its options. Everyone in the workspace sees them, guests included, without installing them (docs: Widgets). The Sticky note, Checklist and Text you add are now widgets too, built the way anyone's can be: data and a drawing, no code.
- New: tool plugins add kinds of tile, drawn by their own code inside the app. `hive tools new | install | remove`; one added is off until you switch it on, since it runs with the app's access. Its tile gets the same `hm` client a view has, held to the permissions it declares, and its settings use the same fields. A tile whose plugin is missing or off shows what it needs; `HIVEMIND_SAFE_MODE=1` starts the app with none loaded (docs: Tool plugins).
- New: three example tool plugins in `examples/tools`: GitHub pull requests (conversation, diff, checks; comment, approve, merge through `gh`), Git history (commits and their diffs; ask an agent to explain one) and a TODO finder.
- Changed: `hive ctl open-tool` opens any tool, added ones included, not only the Browser.
- Changed: the Editor and the Diff tile are one Code tile. Its Changes view is the full Diff tile, comments and send-to-agent included, and opening a file there edits it. Saved Diff tiles open as a Code tile on Changes.
- New: widgets, small live boxes on the board drawn by your own code: put a folder with `widget.json` and `widget.js` in your profile's `widgets` folder and it appears under Board. Guests see it without installing it (docs: Widgets).
- Removed: the "Use hivemind's servers" network choice, whose servers were never run. Reach devices elsewhere through your own server or a VPN such as Tailscale; a device that chose it is on the local network.
- Fixed: an agent no longer keeps showing "approve?" and "needs you" after you allow what it asked; before, it stayed until the allowed command finished, minutes for a long one.
- New: a message one agent sends another (`hive ctl send`, a worker's report, an approval request) reaches a working Claude Code agent at once, read at its next step, instead of waiting for its turn to end. An agent's file says it reads messages typed while it works with `steer: true`; other agents get theirs when the turn ends, as before.
- Fixed: a message from another agent no longer lands in what you are typing to an agent and gets sent with it; it waits until you send or clear your text (Enter, Ctrl-C, Ctrl-U).
- New: a workspace joined from another computer shows in its host's theme (preset, mode, accent, palette, glass, wallpaper), and its views too; your own fonts stay, a wallpaper from the host's files becomes a built-in one, and your workspaces keep your theme (workspace API 0.17: `appearance.get`, `appearance.changed`).
- New: `hive share`, `hive people` and `hive join` work with the desktop app running, not only `hive host`; a `hive join` there opens the workspace in the app.
- Fixed: a guest no longer learns where a shared workspace lives on the host: answers, errors and events name it by its id.

- New: guests open in the host's chosen workspace view, including host-served community views, and can follow the host's live canvas camera.

- Fixed: one failing workspace listener no longer stops a shared workspace's guests from getting updates; connections and a quiet host are logged.
- New: on Android, someone asking to join one of your workspaces while no window of yours is open shows as a notification ("Priya asks to join api"), with Allow and Deny that answer from the notification.
- New: `hive network enrol-link` on your network's server prints a link that puts one more device on it (`--uses`, `--expires`), signed with the admin key `hive-net serve` keeps in its data folder (`--data`, default `/var/lib/hive-net`).
- New: share from a host with no window: `hive share <workspace>` prints an invite link (`--role`, `--uses`, `--expires`), `hive people` lists who is asking and lets them in or turns them away (at another role with `--role`), changes roles and takes people off, and `hive join <link>` asks to join a workspace; each acts through the running `hive host` and lands in its audit log (workspace API 0.16: `people.invite … uses`, `people.answer … role`).
- New: a shared workspace can let in anyone with a valid invite at once, at the invite's role, instead of asking you each time (`hive people rule invite`); with no window of yours open, someone asking to join is sent to your phone (workspace API 0.16: `people.requests`, `people.answering`; push 0.5: `join`).
- Mermaid diagrams in Markdown previews are drawn by mermaid 12: flowcharts, state and class diagrams are laid out by ELK, and use-case diagrams are supported.
- An invalid issue file or `.hivemind/config.yaml` names each field that is wrong and why, instead of printing raw validation JSON; validation messages read as zod 4 words them.
- The app's icons come from lucide 1, which redraws several of them.
- Fixed: notifications in the corner can be swiped away in any direction and a toast that changes size re-measures (sonner 2).
- Fixed: a component's own Tailwind classes and the ones a caller passes merge by Tailwind v4's rules (tailwind-merge 3), so an override written in v4 syntax wins as it should.
- `hive --help` is printed by citty 0.2: plainer layout, and an unknown command's error is shown once.
- Fixed: rejoining an open shared workspace replaces its old connection and role; repeated Join submissions for the same invite ask the host once.

- New: opt into nightly builds from main with `install.sh --channel nightly` or `install.ps1 -Channel nightly`; upgrades and the app's update check stay on the installed channel, while stable remains the default. Roll back with `--version <tag>` / `-Version <tag>`, which installs exactly that build, older ones too; `--channel stable` steps back from a nightly. The 14 newest nightlies are kept.

- Docs: explain sharing over a Tailscale-hosted network and mark the built-in Hosted network unavailable until its server names resolve.
- Fixed: script-entry community views on Android no longer clip their content and controls when the web view resolves a zero page height.
- Fixed: the Share dialog keeps a long invite link inside it, its dropdowns follow the theme instead of showing white, and its buttons line up on the left.
- New: the phone apps have a Views tab: the community views your computers offer the phone, each shown full screen, served by your computer, in your phone's look; anything a view would start or close asks your phone's lock first.
- New: a view can tell whether the screen it is on places the live terminals it asks for (`hm.supports("surfaces")`, view protocol 1.6): your computer's windows do, and a phone will, drawing its own terminal over the view (workspace API 0.15: `view.open … surfaces`; `hive-phone view --surfaces`).
- Fixed: a phone, or a second device, can follow several things on a computer at once. An agent's terminal on your phone no longer stops when you open its chat, a message you send while watching it no longer takes the terminal's output, and a phone reaches more than one workspace on a computer. Each stream a device opens on its connection is now its own: the app and `hive-net` speak a new protocol between them (2) and must come from the same release, as `install.sh` installs them; a mismatched pair stops and says so. `hive-phone agent <workspace> <tile>` follows an agent's terminal and its conversation at once on one connection, and sends it each line you type.
- Build `hive` with Bun 1.4.
- Build with pnpm 12 (`install.sh --dev` and `install.ps1 -Dev` need pnpm ≥ 12); the app's libraries are on their newest patch and minor releases.
- New: `hive-phone push` says which of your devices said no to telling your phone, and why, apart from those that are away (`refused` in `--json`).
- New: your computer tells your phone which agents keep a conversation it can show as a chat: those whose agent file maps their session file (`session.transcript`). The phone offers the chat only for those, and the terminal for every agent (`hive-phone agents --json` says `conversation`; spec/agents.md 0.4).
- New: a community view shown on your phone is served by your computer as its own windows serve it: with the view SDK, through the page that runs a view whose entry is a script, and each file under the same policy (no network, scripts only from the view). It is told your phone's screen size and look, and when either changes (`hive-phone view <workspace> <view> --size 390x844`; workspace API 0.14: `view.list` says the page to load, `view.file` the policy, `view.open` and `view.screen` the screen).
- New: a community view can say it works on a phone (`"phone": true` in `hivemind-view.json`), and your computer offers those to your phone: their files, and the view opened on a workspace there, its host running on the computer, told it is on a phone's screen and doing only what your phone may (`hive-phone views <workspace>`, `hive-phone view <workspace> <view>`; workspace API 0.12, `view.*`).
- New: follow what an agent and you say to each other from your phone, as a conversation rather than its terminal: your prompts, what it says, the tools it uses and what they gave back, as it is written (`hive-phone talk <workspace> <tile> --follow`). Hivemind reads it from the agent's own session file, as the agent's manifest maps its records (`session.transcript`: which records say what, in which fields), so any agent can have it; Claude Code's manifest carries its mapping. Your computer now also remembers which session an agent bound as it started, so that works for agents without a session hook too. After `/clear`, the phone follows the agent's new session: each piece names its session (`spec/agents.md` 0.3), and `hive-phone talk` marks where a new conversation begins.
- Fixed: an agent's terminal you watch from your phone, or from another of your devices, now streams as it comes while the computer's windows are minimized or hidden. Before, its output came in batches of 200 ms then, the delay meant for windows nobody can see, and what you typed echoed only with them.
- New: drive your agents from your phone, not only answer them. Your phone follows every agent on your computers and hosts as it changes: what it is doing, what it waits on, the program it runs and the machine it runs on (`hive-phone agents --follow`). It starts an agent in a workspace's folder with a first prompt, as you would at your desk (your saved options for it, never its unattended mode unless you choose one: `hive-phone start <workspace> <agent> --prompt "…"`), stops its turn with the agent's own keys (`hive-phone stop`), closes it (`hive-phone close`), and shows what it changed in its folder, new files included (`hive-phone diff`). Each is recorded in your computer's audit log as your phone. An agent's file can now say which keys interrupt its turn (`interrupt`); Claude Code's is Esc. `hive ctl keys` takes `ctrl-c`.
- New: your phone knows whose it is. As it pairs, and each time it reaches your computer, it takes your name and colour from your profile there (Settings → Profile); `hive-phone devices` shows them, and a change on the computer reaches the phone the next time it asks.
- New: allow or deny what an agent asks, from your phone. An agent's file can say which keys answer its own permission prompt (`answer: permission: allow / deny`); Claude Code's are `1` (Yes) and Esc (No). Your computer, or a `hive host`, says which waits it can decide, in the list and in the notification, and `hive-phone answer <workspace> <tile> <since> --allow` (or `--deny`) types those keys into the agent's terminal, once, while it still waits on that prompt. An agent whose file says nothing is answered with a line of text, as before.
- New: a push server for your phones, on a network of your own: `hive-net serve --push` (part of `--all`) tells a phone that cannot be reached directly what your computers post it, at its UnifiedPush distributor, or through Apple's or Google's service given your credentials (`--apns-key AuthKey_….p8 --apns-key-id … --apns-team … --apns-topic …`, `--fcm service-account.json`). It passes on only what the devices the phone named have signed, cannot read any of it, keeps none of it, posts into its own networks only where told to (`--push-allow <network>`) and over https on the internet, adds a VAPID token of its own key (the profile's `push.vapid`) for distributors that ask, and tries a busy service once more; beside the access role, only phones on the network register. `access.json` is now readable by the server's user alone, as `push.json` is. Your computers and hosts now sign each notice they post to such a server (the phone's subscription asks them to), a phone on such a network registers there and is told through it, and unpairing a computer from the phone stops the server passing on what that computer posts. The network's profile names the push server (`push`); `spec/push.md` 0.3.
- New: your phone reaches all your computers and hosts, not only the computer it paired with. That computer tells your other devices of the phone, so they let it in (as a phone: it runs nothing there) and tell it what happens there, and tells the phone of them: an agent waiting on you on a `hive host` shows on your phone, on that machine, and notifies it. Unpairing the phone on any of your computers unpairs it on all of them. `hive host status` lists the phones it knows.
- New: reply to an agent from your phone, whatever it is doing: `hive-phone send <workspace> <tile> --text "also add tests"` hands it the line as its next prompt, typed once it is at its prompt, as `hive ctl send` does. `hive-phone watch <workspace> <tile> --type` types into its terminal as you, each line you enter followed by Enter; while someone you let into the workspace holds its keyboard, your phone asks them for it.
- New: your phone says which machine each agent waiting on you runs on ("Nav fix · api on build-box"): your computer, another of your devices, a saved machine, or a guest's computer in a workspace you share, named as your computer knows it. `hive-phone needs` shows it.
- New: your phone tells you when a computer it found away is back. A computer that starts, or wakes from sleep, tells your phone once it is on its network again, and the phone shows it ("desk is back") only if it had found that computer away; a message that comes late, after the computer went away again, is not shown. A computer paired with your other devices or your phone now starts its network as the app starts, so they reach it at once, and again as it wakes. Until the phone apps ship, `hive-phone push --listen` shows it.
- New: pair your phone, or another computer, with a computer of yours from anywhere. On a network of your own, the pairing link lets the device entering it onto that network first, as an invite does: with a voucher for one use on a network that admits only who it is told to, or by registering on an open one.
- New: a phone paired with a computer that is on a network of its own (Settings → Servers) takes that network, and reaches your computers through its relays and lookup server from then on, not only on the same Wi-Fi. On a network that admits only who it is told to, your computer lets the phone in as it pairs; on an open one, the phone registers itself. `hive-phone network` says which network the phone is on.
- Fixed: after changing networks in Settings → Servers, the network started again up to two seconds later, cutting off whatever had begun meanwhile (a phone pairing, an invite). It starts again at once now.
- New: your phone says how many agents are at work on your computers ("Nothing needs you. 4 agents working."), and of a computer that is away, what it last said needed you and when. It can unpair itself from a computer too: `hive-phone unpair <computer>` makes each forget the other, and the computer's audit log records the phone did it. A computer the phone cannot reach is forgotten by the phone alone, and still lists the phone until you unpair it there.
- New: your computer tells your phone when an agent there begins waiting on you, finishes or fails. The phone gives each of your computers it paired with where to tell it (a Web Push subscription: a push service's address, or its own on your network) and every notice is encrypted to that phone alone, so whatever carries it reads nothing of it (`spec/push.md`). Until the phone apps ship, `hive-phone push --listen <address>` takes them in a terminal. Unpairing the phone on the computer stops them.
- New: your phone can ask your computer what needs you: each agent waiting on you there (for a permission, an answer or a plan to review), in which workspace, called what you named it or what it says it is doing, the one waiting longest first. Until the phone apps ship, `hive-phone needs` asks from a terminal; a computer that does not answer is said to be away. `hive-phone watch <workspace> <tile>` shows an agent's terminal as it runs, read-only: a phone never types into one. `hive-phone answer` answers what an agent waits on you for, a line typed into its terminal or its plan approved or sent back with what to change, only while it still waits on that and once: an answer that comes late, or twice, does nothing.
- Fixed (security): in a workspace you share, a guest was told the status of every agent on your computer, in every workspace of yours, with what each said it was doing. A guest now learns about the agents of the workspace they joined, and no other.
- New: pair your phone with a computer of yours. **Settings → Your devices → Use on your phone** shows a QR code for the phone to scan; the phone is then yours, listed under **Your devices** as a phone. It is given a certificate saying so, signed by your person key, and never the key itself, so a lost phone gives none away: unpair it on the computer. A phone runs nothing, so it is never offered as a place to run a frame, open a workspace or move one to, and your computer serves it nothing your other computers get from it (no terminals started, no files or board), only what a phone does: what needs you there, an agent's terminal to watch and what it asks to answer, and where to tell the phone what happens. The phone's core is Rust (`crates/hive-phone`); until the phone apps ship, `hive-phone pair <link>` does what the phone does, from a terminal (pairing 0.3: `spec/pairing.md`).
- New: a frame on another of your devices, or on your `hive host`, shows that machine's files and git: its Diff tile, file tree and editor read and write the folder there, over your network, with no ssh.
- Fixed (security): in a workspace you share, a guest could read files and git history anywhere on your computer, and the board of another workspace of yours, by naming a folder or that workspace where a request names the one they joined. Your computer now answers a guest only about the workspace they joined; your own devices are you, as before.
- New: hand a branch off to a workspace's host. On a frame of yours on your computer in someone else's workspace, **Hand off its branch to the host** (on its machine chip) sends the branch you have checked out there, over your connection to them: it lands in their repository as `handoff/<you>/<branch>`, touching none of their files or branches (handing it off again updates yours, and nobody else's), and a Diff tile on their board shows what it adds to the branch they have checked out.
- New: in someone else's workspace, put a frame on your own computer: choose where it runs (**machine** on a frame, or **add machine**) and **This computer** is offered, with a folder of yours. The terminals and agents you open in it run on your computer, with your logins, in that folder, and everyone in the workspace watches them as they run, at your terminal's size, through the host (your computer is never dialled: it shows them over its own connection). Their keyboard is yours: nobody else types into them, sizes them, or starts, ends or sees anything else on your computer, nor does anything someone else puts in your frame run there. To let the others type into them, choose **Type into it, as its host lets them** on your frame's machine chip (its host's keyboards then go as for its own terminals); with **Run terminals and agents here too**, a terminal or agent that someone who may drive agents there puts in your frame also runs on your computer, as you, in that frame's folder, started with its task by the host and shown in your window, and they may end it. **Only watch what runs here** takes either back at once. On everyone else's board, your frame's machine says whose computer it is (**Priya's computer**), and that you are not connected while you are not.
- New: in a shared workspace, what someone may change on the board follows their role. *Can edit board* moves, sizes, names and groups what is there; placing or closing a terminal, agent or browser tile, changing what one runs, or moving one to another frame is *Can drive agents*; where a frame's tiles run is yours alone. The host takes nothing more from a guest, so nobody starts anything on your computer by editing the board, and a guest's window puts back a change their role does not allow.
- New: in a shared workspace, a terminal says who is typing into it ("Priya is typing", in its title bar), and their pointer is not drawn on the board while they type. A guest's typing into a terminal is marked in the host's audit log once a burst, never what was typed.
- New: a workspace you moved to one of your hosts is still yours to share from your computer. **Share** makes a link that brings people to the host, **People** shows who is in it there and changes their roles or takes them off, and someone asking to join is asked about in each of your windows that has it open, the first answer counting (with none open, they are told no at once). Your computer keeps the workspace's list of people as the host has it, so **Host it here** carries on with whoever was let in or taken off while it was away.
- New: `hive ctl` drives the agents on a machine running `hive host`, as it does the app's. Run there, by you or by an agent in one of its terminals, `hive ctl spawn` starts an agent with no window, in the caller's workspace or the one the host serves, and gives it its task: on its command line where it takes one, else typed once it is ready, a startup screen its launch flags already answered skipped. `read`, `send`, `stream`, `report`, `workflow`, `close`, pipes and supervision work as with the app; the agents' hooks reach the host through the machine's terminal daemon, which still tells your other devices and your phone, and stays up while the host runs. A verb that needs a window (`focus`, `open-review`, views) is refused.
- New: when the host a workspace was moved to cannot be reached, the computer it came from can host it again: **Host it here**, on the workspace's banner, carries on from the board as this computer last saw it. On a network with a lookup server, whoever is in the workspace finds it there by themselves, and the host, once it is back, hands over what changed on it meanwhile.
- New: move a workspace to one of your hosts, so it stays open while this computer sleeps. **Share → Move there** hands a host you paired (Settings → Your devices) the board, notes, people and invite links; whoever is in the workspace follows it there by themselves within seconds, with no new invite, and this computer opens it from the host from then on, as yours. Terminals and agents keep running where they are: those in frames on this computer run here still, reached through the host. **Move here**, on the workspace's banner, brings it back to this computer the same way: whoever is in it follows, and what was done on the host comes with it.
- New: a frame can run on another of your devices. Choosing where a frame runs (**machine** in its header, or **Choose where it runs…** on its machine chip) lists **Your devices**: pick one of its folders, and the terminals and agents in that frame run on that device, in its terminal daemon, reached through your network with no ssh, and keep running when this computer sleeps or quits. A frame on a device shows the device's name and whether it answers. Its files and git are not reached from here yet.
- New: on a network with a lookup server, the computer that shares a workspace says there that it hosts it, and the invite says where to look, so whoever joined finds the workspace's host by the workspace alone (the groundwork for moving a workspace to another of your devices).
- New: pair two of your computers.
