- **Diff: review any branch against any branch — no remote PR needed.** The `branch` tab now has **base … head pickers** — searchable comboboxes that filter local + remote branches as you type (a plain dropdown is unusable on repos with hundreds of branches). Leave them on *auto* for the old behaviour (base → `origin/HEAD`, head → your checkout), or pick any two refs to review an arbitrary branch-to-branch diff — the same merge-base (3-dot) diff a GitHub/Azure PR shows, computed locally with zero host integration. Backed by a new `gitListBranches` git-adapter call + IPC, and a `head` field on the `branch` diff scope; the comment/review surface works unchanged on the result.
- **HTML files open in the in-app browser tile.** Clicking an `.html`/`.htm` file link in a terminal tile, or the new `ExternalLink` icon on an HTML editor tab, opens the file as a `file://` URL in a browser tile in the same frame (spawning one if none exists). Plain `http(s)` links from terminal output route to the in-app browser too. Remote (`ssh://`) workspaces and path-traversal are rejected. Pure routing helpers live in `browser-open.ts` with full unit-test coverage.
- **`hive add mcp` / `hive add skill`** — install one piece of the agentic stack à la carte without re-initialising: `hive add mcp` writes/merges the `hive` MCP server into `.mcp.json`; `hive add skill` drops the `hive-work` claude skill into `.claude/skills/`. Both idempotent.
- **Deterministic "needs you" status via claude's `Notification` hook.** Claude's own permission/question signal (`notification_type`) now drives the tile status — a soft override that lifts an idle tile to `permission`/`question` and auto-clears when the scrape shows work resumed. Hardens the screen-scrape against UI-string drift and off-screen tiles; the scrape stays primary. `idle_prompt` is intentionally not mapped (the scrape already shows idle instantly).
- **Deterministic background-agent status via `SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop` hooks.** Hivemind now injects claude's subagent-lifecycle hooks (alongside the existing Stop / SessionStart / permission hooks), so a tile reads `working` while it has in-flight Task subagents — *including background agents*, where claude's main loop returns to the idle prompt. The signal is event-driven (no screen-scrape), version-proof (a stable hook contract, not a UI string), and correctly attributed (the hook fires inside the spawning tile's own session). Tracked per-tile as a set keyed by `agent_id`, so a missed or duplicate edge can't drift the state. The screen-scrape below remains a zero-config fallback.

### Fixed

- **Claude tiles read `working` during high-effort "thinking" turns.** The status-scrape missed claude's newer working line (a new spinner glyph `✚`, a punctuated task title, and no `esc to interrupt` hint), so a long thinking turn showed idle over the still-visible `❯` prompt. Added the live token-stream counter (`↓ 18.3k tokens`) as the primary signal plus a broadened glyph set. (A deterministic hook-based replacement for the whole working/idle scrape is the planned follow-up.)
- **Agent tiles (codex especially) no longer blink the whole app / lag when focused.** The focused tile on a non-HiDPI display was force-switched to xterm's DOM renderer for crisper text — but a full-screen agent TUI like codex repaints many times per second, and the DOM renderer mutates a DOM node per cell per frame, so at that frame rate it caused window-wide layout/paint storms (the "blink") and lag. Agent tiles now stay on the GPU (WebGL) renderer, which already supersamples for crispness via the DPR override. The DOM crisp-boost remains for plain shells (static output).
- **Terminal tiles no longer leak `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` into the shell.** The persistence daemon runs as electron-as-node and spawned every tile PTY with its own `process.env`, so each terminal carried `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`. Launching any Electron app from a hivemind terminal — hivemind itself, VS Code, Slack — then ran it in node-mode and crashed at startup (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'exports')` in the ESM→CJS preparser). Tile shells now get a sanitized env (Electron-internal runtime vars stripped) on both the daemon and in-process spawn paths.
- **Remote SSH tiles reconnect after closing & reopening hivemind.** On restart the connection pool's in-memory auth map is empty, so a restored remote pty/fs/git connect failed with `All configured authentication methods failed`. The pool now falls back to the keychain-saved (safeStorage-encrypted) credential via an injected resolver, so a remembered host reconnects transparently. (A host you didn't choose to *remember* still needs a manual reconnect; a saved password the keychain can no longer decrypt surfaces the clear "re-enter it" error instead of the opaque auth failure.)
- **Agent tiles now show `working` while a background agent is running.** When claude spawns a background agent, its main loop returns to the `❯` prompt and prints `✻ Waiting for N background agent to finish` — no interrupt hint, no spinner ellipsis. The status detector missed that line and read the visible prompt as idle, so the tile (and its Layers-panel row) showed idle while real work was in flight. It now reports `working` whenever a background agent is pending.

## [1.6.1] — 2026-06-19

### Added

- **`hive init --agentic` now documents the multi-agent control plane.** The installed `CLAUDE.md` gains a "Multi-agent control plane" section covering `hive_spawn_agent` (with `frame`/`supervise`), `hive_send`/`hive_send_keys`, `hive_read`, `hive_approve`, `hive_list_frames`/`hive_list_tiles`, `hive_focus`/`hive_close_tile`/`hive_connect`/`hive_report` — so a spawned agent actually knows it can delegate, supervise, and coordinate other agents. Re-run `hive init --agentic` in a repo to refresh its `CLAUDE.md`.

### Fixed

- **Layers panel: frame headers show a labelled `working` / `needs you` pill, not a bare dot.** The per-frame aggregate (highest-priority state among its agents) now reads as a small pill matching the tile rows, so you can tell at a glance — collapsed or not — whether a frame has an agent working or waiting on you.
- **Layers panel: claude's leading title glyph no longer leaks into the agent name.** Claude Code prepends a `✳` status star to its window title while working; we showed the title verbatim, so it appeared as a stray `·` after the icon. The displayed name is now just the task (our own status pill conveys working/idle).

## [1.6.0] — 2026-06-19

### Added

- **Zen mode — one eye toggle hides all canvas chrome.** A persistent eye button (bottom-left) hides the top tool island, the bottom zoom/nav island, the minimap, and the Layers panel for a clean, full-canvas view (the eye stays so you can restore everything). State persists across reloads.
- **Precise "paused" agent statuses — you can see *why* an agent is waiting.** A blocked agent used to read a misleading scraped "idle". The status bus now carries control-plane-derived states the app knows exactly: **`waiting: review`** (agent blocked handing off a plan, waiting for your plan-review decision) and **`waiting: approval`** (a supervised worker blocked on its parent's `hive_approve`). These are pushed from the source of truth — plan-review tile open → its agent; the approval broker → the waiting worker — and *override* the screen-scrape until the wait clears, so the Layers panel and frame chips show the real reason at a glance (amber, pulsing). Implemented as an override layer on the agent-status bus (`setWaitStatus`) + an `hcp:wait` main→renderer event.
- **`hive_send_keys` — drive a worker's TUI by symbolic keys (answer a native `AskUserQuestion` picker from the supervisor).** A worker's *native* `AskUserQuestion` renders an interactive picker in its own tile and is normally answered by a human there; a supervising agent couldn't pick an option because `hive_send`'s plain-text param can't carry a raw `ESC` byte (arrow keys are `ESC [ B`). `hive_send_keys(tileId, ["Down","Enter"])` maps symbolic tokens (`Up`/`Down`/`Left`/`Right`/`Enter`/`Esc`/`Tab`/`Space`/`Backspace`/`Home`/`End`/`PageUp`/`PageDown`, or any literal text/digits) to the correct terminal bytes **in main**, emitted with a small gap so the TUI registers each — so the supervisor can navigate and select any option. Also `hive ctl keys <tile> Down,Enter`. (Pairs with supervised approvals: broker `AskUserQuestion` to get notified + the options, allow it, then `hive_send_keys` to choose.)
- **Agent-supervised approvals — a spawned worker can escalate its permission prompts to the agent that spawned it (HCP Phase 6).** Spawn with `supervise` (`hive_spawn_agent({…, supervise: true})`, or `"all"`, or a tool list) and the worker no longer stops for a human on a tool-permission prompt — instead the request is brokered to *you* (the spawner): you get a `[hive] APPROVAL — worker <id> wants to run Bash: <cmd>` message and answer with **`hive_approve(reqId, "allow"|"deny"|"always"|"never")`** (`always`/`never` remember the decision for that worker+tool so it stops asking). The worker then proceeds, or adapts to your deny reason. So a delegated worker can run unattended *under your watch* rather than freezing on every prompt. Built by generalizing the plan-review hook: a `PreToolUse` broker hook (injected only for supervised workers, gated on a `HIVE_SUPERVISE` spawn-env that main sets) blocks on the HCP socket until you answer; **fail-safe** — on timeout, no supervisor, or any error it falls through to the normal human permission prompt, never a silent allow. Default brokered set is the mutating/external tools (Bash/Edit/Write/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit/WebFetch); safe reads pass through untouched. Also on the CLI: `hive ctl spawn --supervise …` and `hive ctl approve <reqId> allow|deny|always|never`. Design + prior-art in `docs/hcp-agent-approvals.md`; validated against Claude Code's `PreToolUse` permission-decision contract and the Agent-SDK `canUseTool` / LangGraph-interrupt patterns.
- **Frame targeting + frame discovery for spawned agents.** `hive_spawn_agent` now takes an optional `frame` — a frame id, repo/worktree name, or title (e.g. `'manageark'`) — to choose which canvas frame a worker lands in, instead of always inheriting the caller's frame. Resolution runs most-specific → loosest: exact id → case-insensitive title → worktree/workspace path basename → title substring, falling back to the caller's frame then a new frame. A new `hive_list_frames` tool (and `hive ctl frames` CLI subcommand) lists every frame with its id, title, repo/worktree path, branch, and tile count so agents/CLI can discover targets. `hive ctl spawn` gains a matching `--frame` flag.
- **`hive_list_tiles` now groups tiles by frame and accepts a frame filter.** Instead of a flat tile array it returns `{ frames: [{ frameId, title, repo, branch, tiles: [{ tileId, kind, label, status }] }], loose: [...] }` — so you can see at a glance which agents live in which workspace. Empty frames are omitted; tiles with no/unknown frame fall into `loose`. Pass `frame` (id, repo name, or title — same resolver as spawn targeting) to filter to a single frame, which returns just that group. `hive ctl list` gains a matching `--frame` flag.
- **Spawned agents now auto-report their results back to whoever spawned them.** Previously the only way to get a worker's reply was `hive_read`, which *blocks* up to a timeout and — worse — on timeout returned the raw ANSI terminal buffer (garbled box-drawing bytes), not the agent's words. Now `hive_spawn_agent` defaults to `report: true`: the moment the worker finishes a turn, its **clean reply** (read from Claude Code's session-transcript JSONL via the injected `Stop` hook — never screen-scraped) is delivered straight into the parent agent's session as a `[hive] from <tileId>:` message, and a **visible reporting edge** is drawn from worker → parent on the canvas. So you can fire-and-forget a delegated task and keep working; the answer arrives in your inbox when it's done. This reuses the existing agent-pipe machinery (auto-connecting the worker to its parent). `hive_read` still exists for synchronous use but now returns clean transcript text, and on timeout reports `finalStatus:"timeout"` ("agent still working") instead of terminal garbage. `report:false` opts out for a worker you'd rather poll. (Validated against Claude Code's hook model: `Stop` is the reliable per-turn signal, the transcript JSONL keyed by our injected `--session-id` is the clean source, and PTY stdin is the only supported way to hand a running interactive agent new input — this complements native Task subagents rather than reinventing them.)
- **Diff tile: new "unpushed" review mode** — review code you've committed locally but not yet pushed, beside the existing "working" and "branch" tabs. It diffs `@{upstream}...HEAD` (the net diff of local commits ahead of the branch's remote tracking ref — exactly what you'd push), falling back to the default branch when the branch was never pushed (every local commit is then unpushed). The tab shows the ahead-commit count as a badge and disables itself when the branch is tracked and fully pushed. Built on a new additive `{ kind: "unpushed"; base? }` `DiffScope` and a `resolveUnpushedBase()` resolver mirroring the branch-base logic; the mode toggle is now a data-driven list so future diff sources (a single commit, an arbitrary range, "since tag") slot in with one entry + one scope variant.

### Changed

- **The diff tile's A+/A− font control now scales the WHOLE diff, not just the code glyphs.** The stepper only set `--diffs-font-size` (code + gutter), so bumping the font left the per-file headers and the changed-files tree looking tiny next to large code. The chrome is now derived from the code font (`chromePx = round(font.size × 0.85)`, ~15% smaller — matching the stock 11px-chrome / 13px-code ratio): the per-file header (height, file path, +/− counts, and the viewed/open/discard/stage controls) and the left file tree (`--trees-font-size` + `--trees-item-height`) grow and shrink together. The CodeView's reserved `diffHeaderHeight` tracks the scaled header so sticky/virtualization math stays aligned.
- **The Layers panel is now a DOCKED side panel (t3code / VS Code style), not a floating overlay.** It used to sit *over* the canvas (absolute, glassy island), occluding tile controls near the left edge — which is why it defaulted to hidden. It's now a flex sibling of the canvas: the canvas sits *beside* it and is never covered. Full window height, flat with a right border, **drag the right edge to resize** (width persists, 180–480px), and collapsing leaves a slim icon rail (instead of a floating pill) that still shows the "needs you" / "working" status dot. Defaults to shown now that it can't occlude anything. ⌘/Ctrl+L still toggles it. **Visual pass for a calmer, more modern feel** (Linear/t3-style): taller `h-8` rows with more breathing room, generous `gap`/padding, soft rounded-`lg` inset selection (a gentle fill rather than a hard accent bar), `13px` row text, more separation between repo sections, a borderless airy header, and a panel-collapse icon. **Workspace-UX pass (t3code):** each frame now shows a **per-frame aggregate status dot** — the highest-priority state among its tiles (needs-you > working > idle), pulsing when actionable — so a *collapsed* frame still signals "an agent in here needs you / is working" without expanding it; tile statuses render as tinted **pills** (incl. the new `waiting: review` / `waiting: approval` states).
- **The dev daemon-respawn now keys off the daemon's CONTENT, not its file mtime.** Every `pnpm build` rewrites `out/main/pty-daemon.js` (new mtime), so the app used to think the daemon was "from an older build" and replace it on the next launch — restarting all your live claude/shell sessions, even when the rebuild only touched renderer/main code. The build-stamp is now an FNV-1a content hash of the daemon bundle, so a rebuild that doesn't change daemon code keeps the same stamp and the running sessions are left untouched. (In production this never mattered; it was a constant annoyance during local iteration.)

### Fixed

- **Trackpad scroll no longer "leaks" into a canvas pan at the end of a flick.** Two separate leaks: (1) native scroll-chaining at the inner element's edge — fixed with `overscroll-behavior: contain`; and (2) our *custom* wheel-pan, which `overscroll-behavior` can't govern (it's `preventDefault` + manual viewport math, not native scroll). A trackpad keeps emitting wheel events after your fingers lift (momentum); when the terminal hit the bottom of its scrollback mid-flick, that inertia was read as "nothing to scroll → pan the board", so the content scrolled and then the whole panel lurched. The rule is now **position-independent**: if a tile contains scrollable content (a terminal, or any overflow container), the wheel belongs to that content and *never* pans — at the edge it simply stops. Because there's no scroll↔pan transition at the boundary, the momentum tail has nothing to flip into. Only a tile with no scroller at all pans on wheel-over; panning a scrollable tile is via space/middle-drag or the empty canvas, as before. (Researched against MDN wheel-event docs + the `lethargy` inertia-detection approach; the position-independent rule is simpler and fully eliminates the transition.)
- **Clicking a tile in the Layers panel now focuses it exactly like clicking it on the canvas.** The panel used the older fit-to-screen zoom-out, so navigating to a terminal from the panel landed at a different zoom/position than selecting it directly. It now mirrors the canvas-click decision: text tiles (terminal/shell/diff/editor) snap to 100% with the content-corner clamp; other tiles get the framed focus.
- **Focusing a terminal no longer clips its left edge off-screen.** Selecting a text tile snaps it to exactly 100% (xterm/diff/editor are only pixel-accurate at 1:1), but the old path zoomed to 1 around the *viewport* centre — if the tile wasn't already centred (or was wider than the window) it grew with its left columns (the prompt + line starts) hanging off the left, unreadable. Focus now recentres on the tile, and when the tile is bigger than the viewport it anchors the content corner (left/top) instead of centring, so the readable part is always on screen. (This also unifies two camera controllers that previously fought — the fit-to-tile focus and the snap-to-100% — into one move.)
- **The PTY daemon no longer dies when you close the window on GNOME/systemd desktops.** The daemon is spawned `detached` (its own session, via `setsid`) so it should outlive the window — but GNOME launches the app inside a transient `app-gnome-<name>-<pid>.scope` cgroup whose default `KillMode=control-group` terminates *every* process in the cgroup on close, and `setsid` escapes the process group/terminal but **not** the cgroup. So on every close the daemon (and all your terminal sessions) was killed, defeating the whole point of persistence. The daemon is now registered in its own transient scope via `systemd-run --user --scope` (a `run-*.scope` outside the app cgroup) when we detect we're inside a killable app scope — verified to land in a separate cgroup that survives the window closing. Falls back to the plain detached spawn on non-systemd/non-app-scope environments (containers, plain login sessions, other desktops).
- **Plan review now opens in the agent's OWN frame/project.** The PreToolUse hook hands the renderer the agent's `HIVEMIND_TILE`, which is the PTY id (`hm:<tileId>`), but `openPlanReview` did a raw frame lookup on it without stripping the `hm:` prefix — so it always missed and the review tile landed in the active/first frame. If you planned in one project (manageark) while another (motadata-itsm-server) was active, the review opened over the wrong one. The prefix is now stripped (same seam as `hive_spawn_agent`), so the plan renders beside the agent that produced it.
- **A tile that fails on a stale lazy chunk now offers a real "Reload" instead of a dead "Retry".** After a rebuild, an already-open window references old `assets/<name>-<hash>.js` files; a lazy import (e.g. the plan-review markdown preview) then 404s with "Failed to fetch dynamically imported module" and the tile error boundary's *Retry* just re-requested the same dead hash. The boundary now recognises stale-chunk / `ChunkLoadError` failures and shows a **Reload** button (reloads the window to pick up the fresh build; PTY sessions survive in the daemon).
- **Plan-review tiles now show up in the Layers panel with a "review" status.** A plan-review tile is a live agent blocked on your decision, but it was excluded from the panel — so there was no way to navigate to it and no status indicator. It now appears under its frame with a `▤` glyph and a `review` status, surfaces in the "needs you" filter (a blocked agent is waiting), and clicking it focuses the tile like any other layer.
- **Terminal/tile scroll no longer leaks into a canvas pan.** Scrolling a focused tile (claude, diff, editor, …) used to scroll its content for one notch, then "escape" and pan the whole board once the inner content hit its scroll boundary — which a terminal hits constantly. A focused tile now carries the `nowheel` class so react-flow ignores the wheel over it entirely; the content scrolls and the canvas never pans. (Unfocused tiles still pan on wheel-over as before.) Also fixed a wheel-listener leak where the cleanup's capture flag didn't match the registration. **Follow-up:** the leak had a *second* cause unrelated to react-flow — at the terminal's scroll boundary the wheel's native overscroll chained up to the document and shifted the whole panel. The page root is now `overflow:hidden; overscroll-behavior:none` (it's a fixed-viewport canvas, nothing at page level should scroll) and `.xterm-viewport` is `overscroll-behavior:contain`, so terminal scroll stops at its own boundary. **And the wheel is now scroll-aware:** every tile carries `nowheel` (react-flow never pans/zooms over a tile), and the tile's own wheel handler checks whether the element under the cursor still has scrollback/scroll room in the wheel's direction — if it does, that element scrolls and the canvas does NOT pan; only when there's nothing left to scroll internally (or the tile is at its scroll boundary, or has no inner scroll) does the wheel pan the board. So hovering an *unselected* terminal with scrollback now scrolls the terminal instead of panning the canvas.
- **HCP hardening pass (council-driven).** A 5-agent review (reviewer / architect / code-quality / maintainer / devil's-advocate, run *through* the control plane) surfaced gaps that were then closed: the bare↔`hm:` tile-id mapping is centralized in one `shared/tile-id.ts` seam (was reimplemented in 4+ places); **pipe forwarding now actually fires** (both the `dests()` lookup *and* the write step convert through the `hm:` pty namespace — a verification agent caught that the first pass fixed only the lookup); **agent pipes refuse cycles** at connect time (an `A→B→A` ring would otherwise pump forever once forwarding is live); **dead-tile sends fail loud** (`agent.send` to a non-existent tile now throws `TILE_NOT_FOUND` instead of reporting success); the **spawn-depth gate is enforced** (`DEPTH_EXCEEDED` past depth 3, bounding agent-spawns-agent recursion — previously specified but never checked); **`hive_read`'s `timeout_ms` reaches the wire client** (long reads no longer die at the 130s client default); and the token-less `turn` event is **constrained to real `~/.claude/**.jsonl` transcript paths** (a forged event can't make a read return an arbitrary file's contents). A follow-up P1 pass: the transcript is **tail-read** (bounded 256 KiB window) instead of full-read every turn (was O(n²) over a session); closing a tile now **forgets its pipes + prunes all per-tile control state** (no leaks, no forwarding into a dead tile); and the prompt-submit delay is a single `SUBMIT_DELAY_MS` constant. Remaining P1/P2 items are catalogued in `docs/reviews/hcp-fix-plan.md`.
- **`hive_read` / `agent.send` now address the right tile.** The control surface uses bare tile ids (`tile-…`) but the pty, output recorder, and Stop-hook turn reports are all keyed by the pty id (`hm:tile-…`). `agent.send`/`agent.read`/`agent.stream` now map between them, so a send reaches the agent and a read sees the turn the Stop hook reported (previously `hive_read` timed out even though the worker had replied).
- **Prompts delivered to a spawned/sent agent now actually submit.** The text + Enter were written in a single chunk, which claude's TUI dropped (the prompt sat unsubmitted in the composer). All delivery paths — the queued spawn prompt, `hivemind:send-to-claude`, HCP `agent.send`, and pipe-forwarding — now type the text and press Enter (`\r`) as a *separate* keystroke a tick later (the tmux `send-keys` pattern).
- **`hive_spawn_agent` now lands the worker in the SPAWNING agent's frame**, not a random/active one. The control plane injects the agent's own tile id (`HIVEMIND_TILE`) into its env, the MCP passes it as the caller, and the renderer defaults the new tile into the caller's frame (stripping the `hm:` pty-scope prefix to match; explicit `frame` still wins).

### Changed

- **Dev: the PTY daemon auto-respawns when the build changes.** The daemon now stamps itself with its script's mtime and reports it on `ping`; on launch the app replaces a daemon left over from an older build (sessions persist via snapshots and are replayed by the fresh one). No more manual `pkill -f pty-daemon` after a rebuild to pick up main-process changes (hooks, env injection, HCP). Invisible in production (one matching daemon ships per build).

### Added

- **Clickable file paths in the terminal.** Paths in terminal output — `/abs/file.html`, `./src/x.ts`, `~/notes.md`, `file://…`, and `path:line:col` from stack traces — are now links: clicking opens the file in the OS default app (`.html` → browser, source → editor, image → viewer, dir → file manager) via `shell.openPath`. A new xterm link provider detects them (WebLinksAddon still handles http(s) URLs separately); relatives resolve against the tile's cwd. Safe by construction (hardened per an automated security review): main **confines the target to the tile's workspace** (realpath, so symlinks/`..`/absolute paths can't escape), **allowlists only viewable file types** (so executables, installers, `.desktop`/`.lnk`/`.exe`/etc. are never handed to the opener), rejects extensionless files that carry an execute bit, requires the path to exist, and refuses remote/`ssh://` workspaces. (As before, click the tile once to focus it, then click the link — an unfocused tile's first click just selects it.)
- **Hivemind Control Plane (HCP) — a programmable multi-agent canvas.** A local control plane lets an agent inside a hivemind tile (or a CLI/script) drive the running app: spawn sibling agents on the canvas, converse with them, wire them together, and request human review. The hive MCP gains canvas tools — `hive_spawn_agent` (launch an agent tile + hand it a task → returns a tileId), `hive_send`, `hive_read` (block until it finishes a turn and return its reply), `hive_list_tiles` (with live agent status), `hive_focus`, `hive_close_tile`, `hive_connect`/`hive_disconnect` (pipe one agent's replies into another's input — chain workers), and `hive_open_review` (open a plan in the visual review tile and block for human approve/deny). Turn-completion is **deterministic**, via an injected Claude Code `Stop` hook (not screen-scraping) — replies are read from the agent's transcript. Connected agents are drawn on the canvas with an **animated "data-flow" edge** (a dot travels source → target) so you can see the mesh forming. A new `hive ctl` CLI (`hive ctl spawn|send|read|stream|list|focus|close|connect`) exposes the same surface to the shell, including **`hive ctl stream <tile>`** for live output (an LSP/CDP-style multiplexed subscription over the socket, with backpressure + drop-detection seq numbers). Transport is a 0600 unix socket owned by the app (no network); the socket path + a per-install capability token are injected into spawned agents automatically, and agent spawns are rate-limited (16/min) against runaway fan-out. The MCP's issue tools keep working file-only when the app isn't running. Design validated against tttt / tmux-orchestrator / Google A2A / Claude Code agent-teams — hivemind's angle is making the agent mesh **visible, spatial, and cross-tool**, complementing (not duplicating) Claude Code's native subagents. (Deferred: per-child spawn-depth limits and a remote/devcontainer HTTP adapter.)
- **Plan review** — when a `claude` agent finishes planning and hands off (ExitPlanMode), hivemind opens a native **Plan review** tile on the canvas beside that agent, instead of the agent silently proceeding. Read the rendered plan, mark it up inline — select any text or hover a block to **comment**, **mark for removal**, or drop a **quick label** (Nit / Fix / Question / …), plus whole-plan general comments — and either **Approve** (the agent executes) or **Request changes**, which packages your annotations into structured feedback the agent reads to revise (it stays in plan mode). With no marks, Request changes still takes a free-text note. Auto-installed — no `.claude/settings.json` editing: the PreToolUse hook is injected into the same `--settings` hivemind already manages, and it connects to a local unix-socket bridge (mode 0600, no network). Fails open — if the app isn't reachable the plan just proceeds.

## [1.5.0] — 2026-06-12

### Fixed

- Issues tile stuck on "No workspace" after running `hive init` in an already-open folder. The frame saved `workspaceRoot: null` at open time (before `.hivemind/` existed) and that stale null persisted across restarts. The app now (a) re-resolves the project live when a `.hivemind/` appears in a folder that had none, and (b) on load, re-resolves any frame whose folder gained a `.hivemind/` after it was opened — so the tracker is picked up without re-attaching.

### Changed

- Terminal text selection is now zoom-aware (ported from opencove): clicks map to the right cell at ANY canvas zoom, not just 100%. Because of this, focusing a terminal/editor/diff from the Layers panel or via maximize once again FITS the tile to the screen (zoom to fill) instead of being pinned to 100% — selection still lands correctly at the fit zoom.

## [1.4.2] — 2026-06-12

### Fixed

- Focusing a terminal/editor/diff tile from the Layers panel or via maximize now lands at exactly 100% in a single animation. The panel/maximize paths previously ran two-or-three racing viewport animations (setCenter + fitView + a separate zoom-to-100%), which left the tile mis-positioned and at the wrong zoom.
- Terminal looked blurry after select → unselect → re-select (and sharpened only when you manually zoomed). Selecting snaps the canvas to 100%, but the DOM-rendered text kept its rasterization from the pre-snap zoom. The terminal now repaints after the zoom-snap settles, so it's crisp at 100% without a manual zoom.

## [1.4.1] — 2026-06-11

### Changed

- Terminal tiles now use the Ubuntu / GNOME Terminal default colors — the `#300A24` aubergine background and the Tango ANSI palette — so claude and shell output match the system terminal. Applies to every terminal tile.

## [1.4.0] — 2026-06-11

### Added

- Editor now watches the filesystem. When a file you have open changes on disk (an agent edits it, a git checkout, etc.) a **clean** tab auto-reloads the new content — no more close+reopen. A tab with **unsaved edits** is never clobbered: it shows a non-destructive banner with **Reload** / **Keep mine**. Debounced against agent write-bursts; your own saves don't trigger it.

### Fixed

- Terminal text clipped at the right edge (didn't wrap) after focusing a tile. The WebGL→DOM render swap on focus inherited WebGL's column count, but at DPR=1 WebGL rounds its cell width down and packs more columns than the wider DOM cells fit. `cols` is now anchored to the DOM cell size (re-fit on the DOM swap), so the focused terminal fits and wraps; background WebGL tiles just leave a few px of right margin.
- Terminal/editor/diff selection landed on the wrong row when the tile was focused from the **Layers panel** or via **maximize** (`.`/fit-in-screen). Those paths used `fitView` (variable zoom ≤ 1, or up to 1.6× on maximize), but xterm selection + DOM-text crispness are only pixel-accurate at exactly 100%. Both paths now snap text tiles to exactly 100% (matching canvas-click); non-text tiles keep fit-to-screen.

## [1.3.0] — 2026-06-10

### Added

- Markdown preview in the editor. Open a `.md`/`.markdown`/`.mdx` file → a ◉ Preview toggle appears in the tab; click to render the doc (click again for source). Renders GFM markdown, raw inline HTML (sanitized with DOMPurify), and **Mermaid** diagrams. Themed prose; links open in the OS browser. Footprint-conscious: marked + DOMPurify load only when Preview is opened, and Mermaid's bundle loads only when a doc actually contains a `mermaid` block.

## [1.2.1] — 2026-06-09

### Fixed

- Terminal text selection landed on the wrong row (highlight appeared below the cursor). Focusing a terminal now snaps the canvas to exactly 100% zoom — xterm maps the mouse to a cell using the unscaled cell size, so selection is only pixel-accurate at 1:1. (A prior change fit-to-tiled terminals at zoom ≤ 1 on the now-removed assumption that a DPR supersample kept them crisp at any zoom.)

### Changed

- Selection highlight is now neutral grey instead of warm gold (`--color-select`): the selected-tile ring, layers-panel selected row, and node selection accents.

## [1.2.0] — 2026-06-09

### Fixed

- **High CPU / system crash with active terminals.** The DOM renderer adopted in 1.0.5 re-renders DOM nodes on every frame of output, so many live agent tiles (claude/codex streaming TUI repaints) pegged the CPU and could crash the machine. Terminals now default to the GPU-accelerated WebGL renderer (cheap for high throughput).
- **Terminal text clipping / not wrapping.** An experimental device-pixel-ratio override (a supersample attempt) forced a synthetic DPR onto xterm, which desynced its CSS vs device cell metrics — the WebGL canvas rendered wider than its tile, so TUI lines clipped at the right edge instead of wrapping. Removed the override; xterm uses the display's real DPR, so cell geometry is correct and text wraps.

### Changed

- **Crisp where you look.** The focused terminal now boosts to the DOM renderer (native font hinting → sharp text, like the system terminal) on standard-DPI displays; every other tile stays on cheap WebGL. Because only the selected tile boosts, the heavier DOM renderer is bounded to one terminal — crisp where you're reading, no CPU blow-up. On HiDPI the boost is skipped (WebGL is already crisp). This is xterm's `gpuAcceleration: off` approach (VS Code), scoped to one tile on demand.

### Added

- Per-tile terminal font size: A−/A+ buttons in the tile header and Ctrl/Cmd `+`/`−`/`0`, remembered per tile.
- Render-quality HUD (Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+D) showing the live values that govern terminal sharpness (canvas zoom, dpr, font, will-change/transform, `.canvas-moving`).

## [1.1.0] — 2026-06-07

### Added

- Render diagnostics are now written to `<userData>/render-diag.log` (a new `diagLog` IPC). Every terminal focus auto-appends the render state (zoom, dpr, font, will-change/transform, font-smoothing, whether `.canvas-moving` is active) at t0 / t200ms / settled — so a blurry-text report is readable off disk and over SSH, not just in the on-screen HUD. Self-capping at ~128KB.

## [1.0.7] — 2026-06-07

### Fixed

- Terminal text no longer blurs when a tile is focused/selected. Selecting a tile zooms the canvas to 100%, which briefly applied `.canvas-moving` → `will-change: transform` to the `.xterm`, promoting the DOM-rendered text to a GPU layer that disables crisp rendering. Removed `will-change` from `.xterm` (it was only needed by the old WebGL canvas); paint isolation during motion still comes from the node's `contain`.

### Added

- Terminal render-quality HUD: Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+D toggles a live overlay (on every terminal) showing canvas zoom (and whether it's pixel-perfect 1:1), devicePixelRatio, font size, grid, and the computed styles that affect text sharpness (`will-change`/`transform`/font-smoothing, and whether `.canvas-moving` is stuck). Makes blurry-text reports diagnosable without devtools.

## [1.0.6] — 2026-06-07

### Added

- Terminal tiles: adjustable font size. Ctrl/Cmd `+`/`-` resizes, Ctrl/Cmd `0` resets; the size is shared across every terminal and persisted. Larger glyphs render with more device pixels each, so text is crisper and more legible. Default bumped 12→13.

## [1.0.5] — 2026-06-07

### Changed

- Terminal tiles now render with xterm's DOM renderer instead of WebGL, for crisp, hinted text that matches the system terminal at DPR=1. WebGL rasterizes glyphs into a GPU atlas with no font hinting, so small mono text came out thin/soft; the DOM renderer uses native font rendering. Removes the supersampling CSS-scale hack entirely. (Trade-off: extreme full-screen redraws are slower, irrelevant for agent/shell output.)

### Added

- Diff tile: `diff`/`full` header toggle. Default `diff` collapses unchanged lines and shows only changed hunks (+3 context); `full` expands the whole file. Previously the full file was always rendered.

## [1.0.4] — 2026-06-06

### Changed
- **Terminal text is now crisp at the zoom you actually use.** The 1.0.3 crispness pass only supersampled when zoomed *in* (≥120%), so at the normal/overview zoom most people read terminals at, text stayed soft. Supersampling is now the floor — terminals render at 2× density always (3× when zoomed past ~220%), giving anti-aliased glyphs at overview, ~100%, and zoomed-out. The focused terminal stays 1× so mouse/drag-select mapping is exact. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TerminalTile.tsx`.

### Fixed
- **`hivemind upgrade` failed with "text file busy" while the app was running.** The installer wrote the `hive` CLI binary in place with `curl -o`, which the kernel refuses when the executable is in use (a running app's claude tiles hold the hive MCP binary open). It now downloads to a temp file and atomically `mv`s it into place (same for the AppImage), so upgrades work without quitting first. `install.sh`.

## [1.0.3] — 2026-06-06

### Changed
- **Terminal text stays crisp when you zoom in to read it (level-of-detail supersampling).** xterm rasterizes glyphs at a fixed resolution that the canvas zoom then CSS-scales, so anything but 100% looked soft — and xterm has no per-instance device-pixel-ratio override (confirmed against its rendering internals). Terminals now render at a denser backing (`k×`) when the canvas is zoomed in enough to actually read them, scaling the host + font together so columns/rows are unchanged (no PTY reflow); in overview they stay at `1×` (cheap), so GPU cost is bounded across many tiles. The density level is a single tunable function. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TerminalTile.tsx`.

### Fixed
- **Upgrade blanked the canvas (profile migration silently aborted).** The 1.0.1 migration copied the *entire* legacy profile dir with `cpSync({recursive})`, which throws `ERR_FS_CP_SOCKET` the instant it hits `pty-daemon.sock` (the PtyHost's UNIX socket in userData) — so the whole migration aborted before reaching `Local Storage` and every upgrader started with an empty canvas. Migration now copies only the browser-storage state (`Local Storage`, `Session Storage`, `IndexedDB`, `Local State`, `Preferences`) with a socket-skipping filter. If you already lost your canvas, the old data is intact in `~/.config/@hivemind/desktop/` (packaged) or `~/.config/Electron/` (dev) — copy its `Local Storage` into `~/.config/hivemind/` with the app closed. `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts`.
- **Upgrading while the app was running corrupted/raced the install.** `install.sh` did `rm -rf hivemind-extracted` then re-extracted in place — but `hivemind upgrade` is usually run from a terminal *inside* the app, yanking files out from under the live process. The installer now detects a running instance and **stages** the new version beside the live one (`hivemind-extracted.staged`); the launcher swaps it in atomically on the next start. No need to quit before upgrading. `install.sh`.
- **No app-grid launcher or dock icon after install/upgrade.** `install.sh` extracted the AppImage and made a `~/.local/bin` wrapper but never installed an XDG desktop entry or hicolor icon, so the app didn't appear in the launcher and the running window fell back to a generic dock icon (the bundled icon ships *inside* the AppImage but was never copied out). The installer now writes `~/.local/share/applications/hivemind.desktop` (with `StartupWMClass=hivemind` to match the window's WM_CLASS) + `~/.local/share/icons/hicolor/512x512/apps/hivemind.png` and refreshes the caches, on both fresh installs and upgrades. `install.sh`.
- **Saved SSH host could no longer connect** after the app rename — failed with the opaque `All configured authentication methods failed`. The password is encrypted with `safeStorage`, whose key is tied to the app identity; renaming to `hivemind` orphaned the old key, so decrypt threw and was **silently swallowed**, leaving ssh2 with no credential. Decrypt failure is now detected and surfaced: reconnecting a saved host whose password can't be read drops you into the connect form pre-filled with the host, so you only re-type the password (which re-encrypts under the current key). Also added a clear "no SSH credential available" error instead of the cryptic ssh2 one. `apps/desktop/src/main/remote/{saved-hosts,conn}.ts`, `src/main/index.ts`, `src/renderer/src/components/RemoteConnectModal.tsx`.
- **Clicking an issue in a frame-scoped Issues tile opened an empty "loading…" peek** that never resolved. The card dispatched only the issue id, so the peek re-guessed the workspace root via the prefix→root registry — which misses for repos that were never registered (no MCP run / bind) or that share an id prefix, so the read returned `null`. The Issues tile now passes its own (authoritative) root with the open event, and the peek uses it directly instead of guessing. No data was ever lost — the issue files were always on disk; only the peek looked in the wrong workspace. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{IssuesTile,App}.tsx`.
- **Issue peek no longer hangs on "loading…"** when an issue genuinely can't be read (deleted, or a stale cross-repo link) — `useIssue` settles with `null`, but the peek treated "no data" the same as "still loading". It now distinguishes loading / not-found / error and shows a panel with a Close button. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/components/IssuePeek.tsx`.

### Changed
- **Discoverability / SEO.** Set 20 GitHub repo topics (were none) and a keyword-front-loaded repo description, and added an FAQ to the README that answers the literal search queries ("canvas/GUI for Claude Code", "run multiple AI coding agents in parallel", "tmux for AI agents", "local-first"). Improves ranking on GitHub search, Google, and agentic/answer engines. `README.md` + repo metadata.

## [1.0.2] — 2026-06-04

### Fixed
- **Generic Electron icon in the GNOME dock** (source/dev runs) — the app now names itself `hivemind`, so the X11 `WM_CLASS` matches its desktop entry and the real icon shows instead of the gear. Packaged AppImages already had the icon.
- **Upgrade-safe profile migration.** Naming the app moves its data dir to `~/.config/hivemind`; on first run with the new name the app now clones the most recent legacy profile (`Electron` for dev, `@hivemind/desktop` for packaged) into it, so **upgrading does not blank your canvas** (frames/tiles/layout). Idempotent. `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts`.

## [1.0.1] — 2026-06-04

### Added
- **App icon.** hivemind now has a real icon — two stacked canvas frames holding a lit honeycomb (the canvas-of-frames + the hive), honey-amber on dark. Wired as the AppImage/desktop icon (`electron-builder` `linux.icon`), the live window/taskbar icon, and the renderer favicon; PNGs in `apps/desktop/icons/` + `assets/icon.png`. `apps/desktop/{icons/icon.png,package.json,src/main/index.ts,src/renderer/{index.html,public/*}}`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`.
- **Browser in the tool island.** The top-center tool island now has a **Browser** button (hotkey `7`) alongside terminal / agent / explorer / diff / issues / frame — the Browser tile was previously only reachable via the hidden `7` key or a frame's **+** menu. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{canvas-islands,Canvas}.tsx`.

### Fixed
- **`hivemind upgrade` no longer opens the app** — it just upgrades. New installs intercept `upgrade` in the launcher, but an old launcher (or a bare AppImage symlink) passed the word straight to Electron, which ignored it and opened a window. The app binary now handles `upgrade` itself: runs the official installer, streams its output, and exits without creating a window. `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts`.
- **Diff tile no longer crashes on remote frames** with `CodeView.addItem: duplicate id …`. A malformed/duplicate git path (seen over SSH) produced two CodeView items with the same id, which threw and took down the whole tile. Items are now de-duped by id (first wins, dev-warns) so a duplicate degrades instead of crashing. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/DiffTile.tsx`.
- **Diff tile listed untracked directories as empty `+0 -0` rows.** `git status` collapses an untracked directory into one `dir/` entry, which the diff rendered as an undiffable blank. Status now uses `--untracked-files=all` so each new file is a real new-file diff. `apps/desktop/src/main/git-adapter.ts`.

### Changed
- **Diff tile's changed-files sidebar now uses the `@pierre/trees` `<FileTree>`** — the same component the editor's file tree uses — instead of a bespoke tree. Deep paths (`java/com/flotomate/models/…`) get VS Code-style compact folders, file icons, virtualization, and `⌘P` quick-open; filenames stop truncating. Per-file +adds/−dels show as a row decoration; jump-to-file and toggle-reviewed live in the row context menu. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/DiffTile.tsx`.

## [1.0.0] — 2026-06-04

### Added
- **Settings dialog + agent-browser toggle.** A gear button (top-right) opens Settings with an **"Enable agent browser control"** switch — the opt-in for the CDP bridge, instead of hand-setting `HIVEMIND_BROWSER_CDP=1`. The choice persists to `<userData>/settings.json` and (a debug port can only be opened at launch) offers **Relaunch to apply**; the dialog shows whether the bridge is live this session vs the saved choice, and warns about the loopback-port exposure. `apps/desktop/src/{main/index.ts,preload/index.ts,shared/ipc.ts,renderer/src/App.tsx}`.
- **Agents can drive the Browser tile (`hive-browser` skill).** A spawned agent (in any PTY tile) can now navigate/click/fill/read/screenshot the **visible** Browser tile by extending [vercel-labs/agent-browser](https://github.com/vercel-labs/agent-browser) over CDP. Opt-in via `HIVEMIND_BROWSER_CDP=1`: Electron exposes a **loopback** `--remote-debugging-port` and writes a discovery file (`<userData>/browser-targets.json`, path in `$HIVEMIND_BROWSER_TARGETS`) listing each browser tile's frame + current URL so the agent connects with `agent-browser --cdp $HIVEMIND_BROWSER_CDP_PORT` and picks the right tab. Browser-tile registration now reports `frameId` + `url`. The skill ships into agentic projects via `installAgenticStack` (write-if-absent, like `hive-work`) and uses `npx -y agent-browser` so there's no global install. Skill at `.claude/skills/hive-browser/SKILL.md`. Security: the debug port also exposes the app window, so the CDP bridge is **off by default** and bound to 127.0.0.1 — enable per the skill's instructions. `apps/desktop/src/{main/index.ts,preload/index.ts,shared/ipc.ts,renderer/src/{BrowserTile,canvas-nodes,canvas-node-build}}` + `.claude/skills/hive-browser/`.
- **Browser tile canvas hotkey `7`** — spawns a Browser tile into the active frame, matching the 1–6 tool hotkeys. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{useCanvasShortcuts,Canvas}.tsx`.
- **Browser tile.** A frame's **+** menu now has **Browser** — a full in-canvas web browser with **multi-tab** support (open/close/switch, per-tab favicon + loading spinner + title), an address bar (search or URL), back/forward/reload/stop, **find-in-page** (⌘F), **page zoom** (⌘±/0), **DevTools** toggle, and per-session login persistence (`partition="persist:browser"`). Links/`target=_blank`/`window.open` open as **new tabs** in the same tile (canvas-native tabs) rather than rogue OS windows. Built on Electron's `<webview>` so it lives in the DOM and pans/zooms/clips with the react-flow canvas (a native `WebContentsView` overlay can't). Each tab owns a real `webContents`, so an agent can drive the **visible** tab over the Chrome DevTools Protocol via the new main-process bridge (`browserCdp` / `browser:register`) — navigate, click, read the DOM, screenshot, evaluate; a 🤖 toolbar button runs a live CDP self-test. Guests are hardened on attach (no node/preload). `apps/desktop/src/{main/index.ts,preload/index.ts,shared/ipc.ts,renderer/src/{BrowserTile.tsx,canvas-nodes,canvas-node-build,useSpawn,FrameNode,LayersPanel,tile-kinds,canvas-sizing,canvas-persistence}}`.
- **`⌘/Ctrl+L` toggles the Layers panel** (open or close), forwarded from main so it works even when a terminal has focus. `apps/desktop/src/{main/index.ts,preload/index.ts,renderer/src/{App,LayersPanel}.tsx}`.
- **Worktree sub-frames now nest in the Layers panel** and each repo/workspace frame shows its **current branch** as a Zed-style badge. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{LayersPanel,FrameNode}.tsx`.
- **Opt-in "arrange" for a frame's contents.** Free drag stays the default (and new tiles still auto-pack to avoid overlap), but a frame's header now has an arrange control that snaps its tiles **and** its worktree sub-frames into **Columns / Rows / Grid** on demand — worktrees-as-columns is a clean way to line branches up side by side. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{frame-layout,Canvas,FrameNode}.{ts,tsx}`.
- **Codex + opencode agents.** A frame's **+** menu lists every enabled agent (Claude / Codex / opencode) with its icon; spawning runs that agent's CLI as an agent-terminal and live status detection keys off the command (`detectCodex`/`detectOpencode`). Agents are an extensible **registry** (`agents.tsx`) — adding a future agent is one entry. Codex defaults to a safe sandbox/approval mode; opencode runs bare (config-driven permissions). MVP: no deterministic CLI resume for codex/opencode yet; both use a generic mark until logos are bundled. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{agents.tsx,useSpawn,FrameNode,LayersPanel,Canvas}.tsx`.
- **Claude shows its real logo** (Anthropic's mark) in the tool island, Layers panel, and the send-to-claude picker — via the agent registry.
- **Diff: changed-files sidebar.** A GitHub-style file list with +adds/−dels, per-file **reviewed** checkbox, and click-to-jump. `components`/`DiffTile.tsx`.
- **Send-to-claude target picker.** "Work on this" and the diff "send review" now ask which claude to deliver to when 1+ are open (or spawn a new one), instead of guessing "latest".
- **Editor works in non-git folders** — the file tree falls back to a filesystem walk when the folder isn't a git repo (git decorations just don't show).
- **Remote SSH frames now support password auth** (in addition to SSH agent + private key), incl. keyboard-interactive servers. The connect form has a password field; the password is held in memory only, never written to disk. `apps/desktop/src/{main/remote/conn.ts,renderer/src/components/RemoteConnectModal.tsx,shared/ipc.ts}`.
- **Remote SSH frames.** A canvas frame can now be bound to a directory on another machine over SSH — every tile inside it runs on the remote: the terminal/Claude is a real PTY on the host, the editor reads/writes files over SFTP, and diff/status shell out to `git` on the remote. A frame's "remote" button opens a connect form (host/user/port, SSH-agent or key auth) then an SFTP folder browser to pick the directory. Built on a single pooled `ssh2` connection per host (shared by PTY, SFTP, and git), with TOFU host-key verification and POSIX-escaped remote commands. The remote target is encoded as an `ssh://user@host:port/path` URI that flows through the existing tile-scoping unchanged, so no per-tile rewrites were needed. Design: `docs/design/remote-frames.md`. MVP scope: issues stay local; no live remote file-watching; agent/key auth only. `apps/desktop/src/{shared/remote-uri.ts,main/remote/*,main/{index,git-adapter}.ts,renderer/src/{components/RemoteConnectModal,useWorktrees,Canvas,FrameNode}.tsx}`.
- **Each frame gets its own color.** Repo frames, ad-hoc groups, and worktree sub-frames are now auto-assigned a distinct, stable accent hue (hashed from the frame id, OKLCH at fixed L/C so every hue is equally vivid on the dark canvas) instead of all defaulting to indigo — so workspaces and branches are scannable at a glance on the canvas, in the frame header, and in the Layers panel. Existing frames on the old default migrate on load; the header color swatch still overrides. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{frame-color.ts,useFrameOps,useSpawn,useWorktrees,canvas-persistence}.ts`.

### Changed
- **Command palette (`⌘K`) redesign.** Dropped the loud full-width accent-fill selection and the off-palette shadcn tokens; the selected row is now a soft `bg4` with a brand left-bar + brand icon, default rows muted → full-contrast on select, on the app's own `--color-*` tokens. Refined input, empty state, separators, and shortcut chips. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/components/{ui/command,CommandPalette}.tsx`.
- **Interface-feel checks (make-interfaces-feel-better lens).** Swapped the board card's `transition-all` for the curated `transition` set — `all` would animate layout properties (padding/size) and risk jank; the curated set is color/opacity/transform/shadow only. Added `text-wrap: pretty` to body text (kills single-word orphans) and `text-wrap: balance` on headings (the issue-peek title no longer leaves a lonely last line). `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{views/BoardView.tsx,styles.css}`.
- **Design polish — text-glyph icons → lucide, teaching empty states.** A design critique (i-critique / i-frontend-design lens) flagged two AI-slop tells. Replaced every hand-rolled unicode UI glyph with proper lucide SVGs: the `⋮⋮` drag handle across all five tiles (Issues, Editor, Workbench, Terminal, Diff) → `GripVertical`, and the `▶` play glyph on every "work"/"send" action → `Play` — theme-colorable, consistent sizing, no cross-platform rendering drift. The Issues tile's flat "no issues — ⌘N to create one" / "no workspace" lines became real empty states that teach the interface (icon · headline · one-line hint · a working **New issue** button). `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{IssuesTile,EditorTile,WorkbenchTile,TerminalTile,DiffTile,components/IssuePeek}.tsx`.
- **Issues UI overhaul — accessibility + visual consistency pass.** Audited every issue surface (board tile, detail peek, filter bar, new-issue modal, sidebar) against WCAG-AA contrast, touch-target, focus-state, and design-token criteria, then fixed the lot. Load-bearing metadata (IDs, timestamps, paths, counts, "Unassigned"/"—" placeholders, form labels) moved off the failing `--color-fg3` (#6b7280, ~3.2–4.0:1) onto `--color-fg2` (passes 4.5:1); the `.u-eyebrow` section-label utility was promoted app-wide. Killed sub-legible `9.5px`/`10px`/`12.5px` text in favor of an 11/11.5/12/13 ramp. Added `aria-label`s to every icon-only/glyph-only button (close, ▶work, delete, checkbox, remove-×, search, filter-chips), real focus rings on all inputs/selects/textareas, and `role="checkbox"`/`aria-checked` on the acceptance-criteria toggles. The per-card "▶ work" quick action is no longer hidden until hover; the issue card is keyboard-focusable (Enter opens); the peek's primary "Work on this" is separated from destructive Delete by a divider. Standardized control radius to `rounded-md`, replaced emoji UI glyphs (📁 ✦ ▾) with theme-colorable SVGs in the sidebar. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{IssuesTile,Sidebar,styles.css,components/{IssuePeek,FilterBar,StateMeta,NewIssueModal}}.{ts,tsx,css}`.

### Fixed
- **Terminal text no longer "grows" after a hivemind restart.** On re-attach the PTY daemon keeps the session's original (frozen) cols/rows; the renderer's init `fit()` had already set `term.cols`, so `term.onResize` never fired and the daemon never learned the real geometry — claude stayed stuck at the old (often smaller) size and rendered narrow/oversized inside the tile. The renderer now force-syncs the current `fit()` dimensions on every spawn/attach. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TerminalTile.tsx`.
- **`Ctrl+R` no longer re-opens the `⌘K` command palette.** Recents were folded into the `⌘K` palette but `Ctrl+R` was still wired to "open recent", so both shortcuts opened the same panel. `Ctrl+R` is now swallowed (so a muscle-memory reload doesn't tear down the canvas + re-attach every PTY); recents stay in `⌘K` and File ▸ Open Recent. `apps/desktop/src/{main/index.ts,renderer/src/App.tsx}`.
- **Worktree nesting hardening (architecture + performance review).** Deleting a repo frame no longer orphans its worktree children (they're removed from the canvas; the worktrees stay on disk, detach `×` is the destructive path). A tile dropped inside a worktree frame now reliably joins *that* worktree (its PTY runs on the right branch/cwd) — `parentFrameOf` prefers the innermost frame. Spawning/dragging a worktree no longer makes the nest jump (the separation pass pins the anchor's parent). A worktree frame dragged out of its parent now **detaches** instead of snapping back. Guards against >2-level nesting and double-create races. Tiles and worktree sub-frames share one occupancy model so the parent divides space without overlap. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{Canvas,frame-layout}.{ts,tsx}`.
- **Performance: the branch badge stays off the hot path.** It uses a dedicated long-`staleTime` `git:branch` query (HEAD-gated refresh), not `git:status` — so it doesn't refetch/re-render on every file write during agent work. The selection pipeline returns the node array verbatim when nothing is selected (baked tile zIndex), and the frame auto-fit geometry uses single-pass min/max + capped collision iterations + a consistent dead-band (no self-fire on sub-pixel residue). `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{queries,Canvas,frame-layout}.{ts,tsx}`.

## [0.5.0] — 2026-06-03

### Added
- **Nested git-worktree sub-frames with a Zed-style picker.** A repo frame (the base workspace or a bound workspace zone) now owns nested **worktree** sub-frames. The frame header's new "worktree" control opens a picker modeled on Zed's — it lists the repo's existing worktrees (directory name · branch · short-sha · truncated path) and offers "Create new worktree based on `<branch>`". Attaching or creating one spawns a real nested child frame (react-flow `parentId`) bound to that branch; **every tile inside scopes to the worktree** — terminal/Claude `cwd`, the editor, and the diff all run against `worktreePath`, so each branch's work is isolated on one canvas. The child frame shows a `branch · sha` pill and detaches (removes the worktree + closes its tiles) via ×. Issues stay the project's shared `.hivemind` (a worktree has none of its own). Dragging a repo frame carries its worktree children and their tiles; the parent auto-fits to wrap them. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{Canvas,FrameNode,WorktreePicker,frame-layout}.{ts,tsx}`.

### Changed
- **Whole-canvas frame auto-fit extracted to a pure, unit-tested `computeFrameLayout`.** The geometry math (derive each frame from its tiles, separate overlapping siblings, and now wrap nested worktree children) moved out of the `Canvas.tsx` god-component into `frame-layout.ts` — no behavior change for existing flat frames, fully covered by unit tests. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/frame-layout.ts`.

## [0.4.0] — 2026-06-03

### Added
- **`hivemind upgrade` / `hive upgrade` — self-update to the latest release.** Re-runs the official installer (downloads the newest GitHub release if newer, no-op if already current; `--dev` rebuilds a source install). Works as a `hive` CLI subcommand and as `hivemind upgrade` on the desktop launcher. `apps/cli/src/commands/upgrade.ts`, `install.sh`.

### Fixed
- **Pressing `.` (or Escape) while renaming a tile triggered focus-mode instead of typing.** The canvas focus hotkeys intentionally fire even over a terminal/editor, but they also hijacked real text fields — so a `.` in the tile-rename input (or palette/forms) jumped to focus-mode. Those hotkeys now skip genuine text inputs (`<input>` / non-terminal `<textarea>`) while still working over the terminal/editor. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.

## [0.3.2] — 2026-06-03

### Fixed
- **Terminal text selection (and clicks) landed on the wrong row when zoomed.** xterm maps the mouse to a cell using its *unscaled* cell size, but react-flow CSS-scales the canvas at any zoom ≠ 100% — so selecting a line grabbed one 2–3 rows off (a known xterm limitation under CSS transform; it also threw off link clicks and mouse-reporting clicks). Selecting a terminal tile now snaps the canvas to exactly 100%, where the mouse→cell math is pixel-accurate, so selection, link clicks, and mouse clicks all hit the right row. (Editor/diff tiles are unaffected — they use DOM coordinates that scale correctly.) `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.
- **Clicking a URL in the terminal now opens it in the browser.** The terminal had no link handling at all — URLs were only openable via an agent's own mouse-reporting, which is unreliable. Added xterm's WebLinksAddon (plain `http(s)` URLs become clickable) plus a `linkHandler` for OSC 8 hyperlinks (which claude emits); a click routes through `window.open` → the existing `setWindowOpenHandler` → `shell.openExternal`, opening in the OS browser (http/https only, no popup). `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TerminalTile.tsx`, `package.json`.
- **Image paste into claude was broken (regression in 0.3.1).** The clipboard work in 0.3.1 added a Cmd/Ctrl+V handler that called `preventDefault()` and pasted clipboard **text only** — so an image on the clipboard was swallowed and never reached claude (which reads images from the system clipboard on paste). Paste is no longer intercepted at all; the copy shortcut stays. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TerminalTile.tsx`.

## [0.3.1] — 2026-06-02

### Changed
- **Spawning honors the current selection instead of always asking.** With 2+ frames, opening a tile (claude/shell/diff/…) from the ToolIsland, palette, or hotkey used to always pop the workspace picker. Now a selected frame — or the frame holding the selected tile — IS the target, so it spawns straight in; the picker only appears when nothing is selected to disambiguate. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.
- **Crisper terminal text — especially when a tile is selected.** xterm rasterizes its glyphs to a canvas that the pan/zoom viewport then CSS-transforms; a fractional pan offset or an off-by-epsilon zoom lands that bitmap on sub-pixels and looks blurry, and it got *worse* on select because selecting promotes the tile to its own compositing layer (the known xyflow#3282 "viewport transform not rounded to integers" issue). The live viewport transform now snaps to whole device pixels (and a near-1 zoom to exactly 1.0) whenever it settles — after a pan, after a fling, and on tile select — so text is sharp at rest and selecting a tile no longer fuzzes it. (Zoom levels other than 100% still scale the bitmap — inherent to canvas content.) `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.

### Changed
- **Sharper terminal glyphs (xterm rendering knobs).** Enabled `rescaleOverlappingGlyphs` (glyphs that overflow their cell — powerline, wide Unicode — are rescaled to fit instead of clipping/smearing) and made `customGlyphs` explicit (box-drawing/block characters drawn as crisp vectors, not font bitmaps). xterm 5.5 has no option to render above the display's device-pixel-ratio, so this plus the self-hosted font + atlas rebuild is the realistic ceiling for crispness. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TerminalTile.tsx`.

### Fixed
- **Terminals had no copy/paste, and Ctrl/Cmd+wheel didn't zoom over a tile.** xterm doesn't copy on its own (Ctrl+C just sends SIGINT), so there was no way to copy terminal text. Added the standard rule: **Cmd/Ctrl(+Shift)+C copies the selection** (and clears it, so a second press still interrupts); plain Ctrl+C with nothing selected still sends SIGINT; **Cmd/Ctrl(+Shift)+V pastes** (bracketed-paste aware). Separately, **Ctrl/Cmd+wheel now zooms the canvas even when the cursor is over a selected tile** — it's handled in the capture phase so it beats the terminal's own scroll instead of being swallowed by it. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{TerminalTile,Canvas}.tsx`.
- **Blurry / mis-weighted terminal & code text (wrong font).** The terminal font (JetBrains Mono) was loaded from the Google Fonts CDN via a `@import`, which is async — and xterm measures its font at open and builds a glyph atlas from whatever is available *then*. With the web font not yet loaded it built the atlas on the fallback font's metrics and never corrected, so terminals rendered slightly blurry/mis-spaced (and on a cold/offline launch the real font never arrived at all). JetBrains Mono is now **self-hosted** (bundled via `@fontsource`, offline-safe), and each terminal **rebuilds its glyph atlas once the font is actually loaded** (re-fit + clear the WebGL texture atlas + refresh), so glyphs render at the correct metrics. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{main.tsx,TerminalTile.tsx,styles.css}`, `package.json`.
- **Diff / file tree showed gitignored files that were already tracked.** `.gitignore` only affects *untracked* files — a file committed before being added to `.gitignore` keeps showing in `git status`/`diff`, and plain `git check-ignore` reports a tracked path as "not ignored". The diff and file-tree now filter their file lists through `git check-ignore --no-index`, which applies the ignore rules regardless of tracking, so a gitignored-but-tracked file (e.g. a committed `config.json` you later added to `.gitignore`) no longer clutters the diff. Untracked-ignored files were already excluded; this closes the tracked case. `apps/desktop/src/main/git-adapter.ts`.
- **One crashing tile no longer blacks the entire app.** Tiles are arbitrary React (xterm, the pierre diff `CodeView`, CodeMirror) and any one can throw during render — with no error boundary a single throw unmounts the whole React tree, so the entire canvas goes black (hit in practice by a diff tile). Because the layout persists, that tile reopened on every launch and re-blacked the app. Each tile is now wrapped in a `TileErrorBoundary`: a crash is isolated to that one tile (shown as a readable fallback with the error + Retry/Close), the rest of the canvas keeps working, and live PTY sessions are untouched (they live in the daemon). `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TileErrorBoundary.tsx`, `Canvas.tsx`.

## [0.3.0] — 2026-06-02

### Added
- **Tiles show the agent's own session title, and notifications name the workspace.** Agents set the terminal window title (OSC 0/2) to a live summary of what they're doing — claude writes a task title like "Refactor auth". hivemind now reads it (via xterm's title event) and shows it as the tile's session name on the header, the canvas session chips, toasts, and OS notifications, instead of a generic "claude #2". A user rename still wins and persists; the agent title is a live, non-persisted overlay. OS notifications also include the **frame (workspace) name** so a background ping tells you *which* project's agent wants you. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{TerminalTile,Canvas,agent-state}.tsx`, `src/main/agent-notify-core.ts`, `src/shared/ipc.ts`.
- **Native OS notifications when an agent needs you or finishes.** When the hivemind window is unfocused, a desktop notification fires the moment any agent tile (all 15 detected CLIs, not just claude) transitions to a needs-you state (permission/question/blocked) or finishes a task (working→idle). Clicking it restores + focuses the window and flies the canvas to that tile; the window also flashes its taskbar entry until you look. The notification is driven off the existing in-app agent-status bus — one state machine, two surfaces (in-app toast when the window is focused but the tile isn't selected; OS popup when you're away) — so it is transition-deduped and covers every agent. `apps/desktop/src/main/{agent-notify,agent-notify-core}.ts`, `src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.

### Fixed
- **"▶ Work on this" often did nothing (or prompted the wrong agent).** The button spawned a fresh claude tile and then fired the work prompt on a blind `setTimeout(2500)` aimed at the "latest" claude. Two failures: with 2+ workspaces the spawn first opens a frame **picker** (no tile yet), so the 2500ms send landed on an *old* claude or vanished; and even single-frame, 2.5s routinely lost the race against claude+MCP startup, typing the prompt before the input was ready. The prompt is now **attached to the spawned tile** and the tile delivers it to itself the first moment it reaches a ready input prompt (one-shot, via the agent-status detection) — it survives the picker and never races startup. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{claude-bus,Canvas,TerminalTile}.tsx`, `IssuesTile.tsx`, `components/IssuePeek.tsx`.
- **False/missed agent notifications.** Two detection bugs undermined the notifications above: (1) the per-tile agent-status poll skipped entirely while the window was hidden/minimized — exactly when an OS notification matters — so finishes/prompts went unnoticed when you were away; it now keeps scanning (cost stays bounded by the existing output-dirty gate). (2) Claude briefly shows its idle prompt *between* tool calls, which the screen scrape misread as "finished," firing a premature completion notification; a working→idle flip is now held until Claude has been genuinely quiet across a confirming scan. The previous claude-only `Notification`-hook log path (delayed, single-agent) is removed in favour of the unified bus. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{TerminalTile,agent-state}.tsx`, `src/main/{claude-resume,pty-daemon}.ts`.
- **Scrolling inside a selected tile (claude / diff / editor) didn't work.** The per-tile wheel handler ran in the CAPTURE phase and called `stopPropagation()` for a selected tile — which stops the event before it ever reaches the terminal/editor, so xterm scrollback and claude's mouse-reporting scroll got nothing. The handler now binds in the BUBBLE phase when the tile is selected (content scrolls first, then react-flow is blocked from also panning) and only uses the capture phase when unselected (to intercept the wheel before the content and pan the canvas). Affects every tile type via the shared `useTileWheelZoom`. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.

## [0.2.0] — 2026-06-01

### Fixed
- **Spawning a tile in a new frame disturbed existing claude sessions.** Off-viewport node culling (`onlyRenderVisibleElements`, on past 8 nodes) UNMOUNTS culled nodes — and our tiles wrap live PTY sessions. Each spawn recenters the viewport onto the new tile, pushing existing claude tiles off-screen → they got culled → their PTY was torn down (detach + xterm/WebGL rebuild + replay, which reads as "the session restarted") in daemon mode, or outright killed and respawned (a genuinely NEW claude session) in the in-process path. Culling is now disabled — every terminal node stays mounted regardless of viewport (xterm's WebGL addon already falls back to the DOM renderer if the GPU context cap is hit). Regression e2e: 12 tiles stay mounted past the old cull threshold and across a hard pan. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.
- **claude session resume was unreliable with multiple frames.** Each claude tile records its live session id (to follow `/resume` switches) via a SessionStart hook. The old hook did an unsynchronized read-modify-write on a single shared `tile-sessions.json` through one fixed `.tmp` path — so when several frames' claude tiles fired their hooks at once on restart, writers lost each other's updates (reproduced: 7/24 survived) and a tile's tracked id vanished, breaking its resume. Tracking is now one file per tile (no shared state, unique tmp), so concurrent tiles can't clobber each other. The legacy map is still read as a migration fallback. `apps/desktop/src/main/tile-session-store.ts`.
- **A claude tile could VANISH when resuming a session whose history was gone.** When `claude --resume <id>` hit a missing JSONL, the daemon retries once with `--session-id` (fresh session, same id) so the tile survives. But the retry kills the old PTY and respawns in place, and the old PTY's kill-induced `exit` then ran the teardown path with no check that it was still the session's current PTY — deleting the just-retried live session. PTY `data`/`exit` handlers now ignore events from a PTY that's already been replaced. Caught by a new end-to-end integration test. `apps/desktop/src/main/pty-session-manager.ts`.
- **Upgrading over an old install left `hivemind` broken (self-exec launcher / `ENOENT`).** Two installer bugs compounded: (1) `write_launcher` did `cat > "$BIN_DIR/hivemind"` without first removing an existing launcher — and old installs left `~/.local/bin/hivemind` as a **symlink** to the extracted `AppRun`, so `cat >` followed the symlink and wrote the wrapper *into the genuine AppRun*, turning it into a script that execs itself (infinite loop, app never starts); (2) even with that fixed, the bundled AppRun auto-detects `APPDIR` by searching for `$1` as a file, but `$1` is `--no-sandbox`, so `APPDIR` collapsed to empty and it tried to exec `/hivemind` (`No such file or directory`). `write_launcher` now `rm -f`s the old launcher first (never writes through a symlink) and the generated wrapper exports `APPDIR` explicitly. The version-skip self-heal also detects an already-corrupted AppRun (missing the genuine `BIN=` marker) and re-extracts it from the AppImage, and always refreshes the launcher to the working format. Re-running `install.sh` now repairs machines the old installer broke. `install.sh`.

### Changed
- **Extracted the claude resume/tracking transforms into `claude-resume.ts`** (electron-free) so the resume logic is unit-testable. New integration test drives the real `SessionManager` + transforms + tracker `.cjs` with a faithful fake `claude`: two frames, `/resume` an old session in one + a new session in the other, snapshot, restart the daemon, and verify each tile resumes the correct session (incl. the `/resume` switch surviving restart) and that a missing-history resume recovers without losing the tile. `apps/desktop/src/main/{pty-daemon,claude-resume}.ts`, `apps/desktop/tests/unit/{tile-session-store,claude-resume.integration}.test.ts`.

### Added
- **Cross-repo issues — move, copy, and link issues across workspaces (and let agents manage multiple repos).** A new global **workspace registry** (`~/.config/hivemind/registry.json`, shared by the app, `hive` CLI, and MCP) maps each workspace prefix → its `.hivemind` root. Because an issue id like `MANAGEARK-3` already names its workspace via the prefix, a full id is its own cross-repo address — no extra qualifier. Workspaces auto-register on `hive init`, on opening/binding a project, and when the MCP server starts.
  - **Issue panel (IssuePeek): a Relations section** — link chips (click to open the linked issue in its own repo, × to unlink), a "+ Link issue" form (`relates`/`blocks`/`blocked-by`/`duplicates`/`parent-of`/`child-of`, reciprocal recorded automatically on the other end), and a **Transfer** control to **Copy** (source kept, both linked with `relates`) or **Move** (source deleted, new issue stamped `moved-from`) into another registered workspace. Move reopens the new issue in its destination.
  - **CLI:** `hive workspace list|register`, `hive move <ID> <PREFIX> [--copy]`, `hive relate <ID> <OTHER-ID> [--type ..] [--remove]`.
  - **MCP (agent multi-repo management):** every id-addressed tool is now cross-repo aware (an id whose prefix belongs to another registered repo resolves automatically), plus `hive_list_workspaces`, `hive_move_issue`, `hive_link_issue`, and a `workspace` filter on `hive_list_issues`. `hive_create_issue` gains `acceptance_criteria` so agents write structured criteria instead of cramming them into the description.
  - Move refuses an issue with sub-issues (would orphan their files). Files stay the source of truth; the registry is a lazily-pruned index. `packages/hive-core/{registry,cross-repo}.ts`, `packages/hive-mcp/src/index.ts`, `apps/cli/src/commands/{workspace,move,relate}.ts`, `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/components/IssuePeek.tsx`.

### Fixed
- **Cross-repo issue leak — a frame bound to a repo with no `.hivemind` showed another repo's issues.** Issues/diff/tree tiles inside a workspace zone fall back to the canvas's base root only when the zone is a *worktree* (same repo, other branch). For a bound *workspace* zone whose repo has no `.hivemind`, the tile now scopes to `root: null` (empty board) instead of inheriting the launch repo's root — which had been rendering, e.g., the `manageark` board inside a frame labelled `motadata-itsm-server`. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/Canvas.tsx`.
- **`installAgentic` no longer throws for a repo without a workspace.** Binding/switching to a repo that has never been `hive init`'d fired a best-effort `installAgentic` that threw `no .hivemind workspace at …`, surfacing a noisy main-process handler error for an entirely expected state. It now returns `{ ok: false, reason: "no-workspace" }`. `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts`.
- **Agent-written acceptance criteria now show in the Acceptance Criteria panel.** `hive_create_issue` only takes free-text `description`, so agents embed the checklist under a plain `Acceptance criteria:` label line (not a `## ` heading) inside the description — the parser only recognised a markdown heading, so those items stayed buried in the description and the dedicated panel showed empty. `parseSections` now also splits on a plain or bold `Acceptance criteria:` label; `serializeSections` rewrites it to a canonical heading on next save. `packages/hive-core/src/storage.ts`.

## [0.1.3] — 2026-06-01

### Performance
- **Agent status polls pause when the window is hidden.** Each active agent tile scans its screen every 1.2s to update its status dot; with `backgroundThrottling` off (so PTY output keeps flowing) those scans ran even while the window was minimized, with no UI to update. They now skip when `document.hidden`. `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/TerminalTile.tsx`.
- **Heavy tiles load on demand (faster startup).** The diff tile (`@pierre/diffs` + its ~1.25MB WASM/cpp syntax engine) and the workbench editor (CodeMirror) are now `React.lazy` code-split, so a terminal-only session no longer pays to parse/compile them at launch — they fetch their chunk on first open (with a small "Loading…" placeholder). The WASM/cpp grammar chunks are fully out of the startup path now.
